Quasimorphisms on subshifts and their cohomology

Given
{A}
a finite set (the alphabet) and
R:{A}\times{A}\to\{0,1\}
consider the one-sided shift

\Sigma=\Sigma(R)=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}=(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:x_{n}\in{A},R(x_{n},x_{n+1})=1,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\}.

As in
Definition

2.14
we identify
{A}=\{1,\cdots,d\},R=(R_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}\in\Mat_{d}{\{0,1\}}
, and suppose that
R
is irreducible and aperiodic. The product topology on
\Sigma
is induced by the metric

\distop_{\Sigma}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})=\frac{1}{2^{N(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})+1}},\quad N=\max\{n:x_{i}=y_{i},i=0\ldots N\}.

The set of finite allowed words is
{W}={W}_{R}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}{W}_{n}
, where

\displaystyle{W}_{n}=\begin{cases}\{*\}\quad(*\text{ is the empty word})&n=0\\
\{\bm{a}\in{A}^{n}:\exists\ \mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma,x_{i}=a_{i-1}\ \forall 0\leq i\leq n-1\}&n\geq 1.\end{cases}

We also write
{W}_{\leq n}=\bigcup_{k=0}^{n}{W}_{k}
. If
\bm{a}\in{W}
we define the quantity
|\bm{a}|
to be
n
if and only if
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n}
;
|\bm{a}|
is the length of
\bm{a}
. Given finite words
\bm{a}=a_{1}\cdots a_{n},\bm{b}=b_{1}\cdots b_{m}\in{W}
of lengths
n,m\geq 1
and such that
R(a_{n},b_{1})=1
the concatenated word
\bm{a}\bm{b}
is,

\bm{a}\bm{b}=a_{1}\cdots a_{n}b_{1}\cdots b_{m}.

Concatenation with the empty word is defined in the obvious way. It is direct to check that
\bm{a}\bm{b}\in{W}
.

Definition 4.1
.

A word
\bm{a}\in{W}\setminus\{*\}
is said to be periodic if
R(a_{|\bm{a}|},a_{1})=1
. For a such a word we let
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a})=\bm{a}\bm{a}\bm{a}\cdots\in\Sigma
, and write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})=\{\bm{a}\text{ is periodic and }|\bm{a}|\mid n\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}).

If
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),n\geq 1
we denote
\bm{a}^{n}=\underbrace{\bm{a}\cdots\bm{a}}_{n\text{ times}}
, and set
\bm{a}^{0}=*,\forall\bm{a}\in{W}
. The shift map on
\Sigma
is the continuous endomorphism
\tau:\Sigma\toit
,
\tau(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=(x_{n+1})_{n\geq 0}
.

Remark 4.2
.

If
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
then
\tau^{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a})
. We will write

\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}):\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\},

and likewise
\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)
, the set of periodic points of
\tau
.

Lemma 4.3
.

It holds

(1)

\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)
is dense in
\Sigma
.

(2)

If
i,j\in{A}
, then there exists
\bm{u}^{(ij)}\in{W}_{M-1}
so that
i\bm{u}^{(ij)}j\in{W}
. As a consequence, for every pair of words
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{W}
there exist
\bm{u},\bm{v}\in{W}_{M}
so that
u_{1}=a_{1},v_{1}=b_{m}
and
\bm{a}\bm{u}\bm{b}\bm{v}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
.

This is a direct consequence of irreducibility. See Lemma
1.3
in
[
14
]
.

For
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},n\geq 1
the cylinder in
\Sigma
corresponding to this word is

(4.1)

[\bm{a}]=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma:x_{i}=a_{i},0\leq i\leq n-1\},

and for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in[\bm{a}]
we also denote
[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}=[\bm{a}]
. Observe that

[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\Sigma:\distop_{\Sigma}(\tau^{i}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\tau^{i}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})<1/2,i=0,1,\ldots n-1\};

the set
[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}
will be referred to as the
n^{th}-
Bowen ball of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
. To conclude, we write
{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
for the set of Borel probability measures
\mu
on
\Sigma
that are invariant under
\tau
(meaning,
\mu(A)=\mu(\tau^{-1}A)
for every Borel set
A
). We remind the reader that
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
is ergodic if every Borel set
A
satisfying
A=\tau^{-1}(A)
also verifies
\mu(A)\mu(A^{c})=0
.

Here are the main definitions of this part.

Definition 4.4
.

For
L:{W}\to\mathbb{R}
denote

\displaystyle\delta L(\bm{a},\bm{b})=L(\bm{a}\bm{b})-L(\bm{a})-L(\bm{b})

\displaystyle|\delta L_{n,m}|=\max\{|\delta L(\bm{a},\bm{b})|:|\bm{a}|=n,|\bm{b}|=m\}

\displaystyle\lVert\delta L\rVert=\sup_{n,m}\{|\delta L_{n,m}|\}.

L
is a quasimorphism if
\lVert\delta L\rVert<\infty
: in this case
\lVert\delta L\rVert
is the defect of
L
. The set of quasimorphisms is denoted by
\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
.

\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
forms naturally a vector space and
L\mapsto\lVert\delta L\rVert
is a semi-norm on it; moreover
\lVert\delta L\rVert=0
if and only if for every pair of concatenable words
\bm{a},\bm{b}
,

L(\bm{a}\bm{b})=L(\bm{a})+L(\bm{b})
.

From the discussion in the last part of the previous session it follows that we are interested in quasimorphisms that are defined in principle only on periodic points of
\tau
. To make this precise we rely on the second part of
Lemma

4.3
.

Definition 4.5
.

If
\bm{a}\in{W}
we denote by
\widetilde{\bm{a}}
any word of the form
\bm{a}\bm{u}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, with
\bm{u}\in{W}_{\leq M}
.

For
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
we denote by
\bm{c}=\bm{a}\star\bm{b}
any word of the form

\bm{c}=\bm{a}\bm{u}\bm{b}\bm{v}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathcal{W}),

where
\bm{u},\bm{v}\in{W}_{\leq M}
.

Remark 4.6
.

By
Lemma

4.3
, if
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\bm{b}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{m}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
then there exists
\bm{a}\star\bm{b}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+m+2M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
.

The next step is to restrict the theory from arbitrary admissible words to periodic words, which are the symbolic counterparts of periodic orbits.

Definition 4.7
.

By a quasimorphism on
\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
we mean a map
L:\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathbb{R}
verifying: given
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{W}
, it holds

\lVert\delta L\rVert\coloneqq\sup\left\{|L(\bm{c})-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})|:\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{W},\widetilde{\bm{a}},\widetilde{\bm{b}}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\bm{c}=\bm{a}\star\bm{b}\right\}<\infty.

The set of such functions is denoted by
\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
, and we call
\lVert\delta L\rVert
the defect of
L
.

Note that
r:\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})),r(L)=L|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}
verifies

\lVert\delta r(L)\rVert\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert+2\lVert L|_{{W}_{\leq M}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}.

We equip
\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
with the norms

(4.2)

\displaystyle\lVert L\rVert=\lVert L|_{{W}_{\leq M}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}+\lVert\delta L\rVert

(4.3)

\displaystyle\lVert L\rVert=\lVert L|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}_{\leq M})}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}+\lVert\delta L\rVert,

and leave it to the reader to verify that these are complete norms (see
Propositions

2.8
and
3.1
).

As in the case of groups, we will be concerned with cohomology classes of quasimorphisms.

Definition 4.8
.

The quasimorphism
L
is said to be cohomologically trivial if
\lVert L|_{{W}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}<\infty
. Two quasimorphisms
L,L^{\prime}
are cohomologous (
L\sim L^{\prime}
) if their difference is cohomologically trivial.

We write
[L]:=\{L^{\prime}:L^{\prime}\sim L\}
for the cohomology class of
L
, and denote

\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}{\ell^{\infty}({W})}.

Similarly,

\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))}{\ell^{\infty}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))}.

It is clear that
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
are vector spaces over
\mathbb{R}
. We equip them with the (Banach) norm

(4.4)

\displaystyle\lVert[L]\rVert:=\inf\{\lVert L^{\prime}\rVert:L^{\prime}\sim L\}.

The following holds true.

Proposition 4.9
.

The spaces
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
are Banach isomorphic.

Fix
s:{W}\to\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
so that
s(\bm{a})=\widetilde{\bm{a}}=\bm{a}\bm{u}
, where
\bm{u}\in{W}_{\leq M}
, and furthermore
s(\bm{a})=\bm{a}
if
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. Given
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
define
\widetilde{L}(\bm{a})=L(s(\bm{a}))
. It is direct that
\widetilde{L}\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, with
\lVert\delta\widetilde{L}\rVert\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert
,
\lVert\widetilde{L}|_{{W}_{\leq M}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq\lVert L|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}_{\leq M})}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}
.

Clearly
L\sim L^{\prime}
implies
\widetilde{L}\sim\widetilde{L}^{\prime}
, and conversely. From here one constructs a continuous linear bijection between the two Banach spaces
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
, hence a continuous isomorphism.
∎

4.1.
Basic properties of quasimorphisms

For later use, in this part we establish some properties of quasimorphisms and introduce notation. Fix
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\{\bm{a}_{1},\cdots,\bm{a}_{k}\}\subset{W}\setminus\{*\}
be such that
\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k}\in{W}
. Then

|L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k})-\sum_{i=1}^{k}L(\bm{a}_{i})|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\left|\delta L_{\sum_{i=1}^{j}|\bm{a}_{i}|,\sum_{i=j+1}^{k}|\bm{a}_{i}|}\right|\leq(k-1)\lVert\delta L\rVert.

We argue by induction on
k
. For
k=2
the claim is immediate from the definition. Assume it holds for
k-1
. Then

\displaystyle|L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k})-\sum_{i=1}^{k}L(\bm{a}_{i})|

\displaystyle=\lvert L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k})-L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k-1})-L(\bm{a}_{k})+L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k-1})-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}L(\bm{a}_{i})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left|\delta L_{\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}|\bm{a}_{j}|,\,|\bm{a}_{k}|}\right|+|L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k-1})-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}L(\bm{a}_{i})|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\delta L_{\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}|\bm{a}_{j}|,\,|\bm{a}_{k}|}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{k-2}\left|\delta L_{\sum_{i=1}^{j}|\bm{a}_{i}|,\sum_{i=j+1}^{k-1}|\bm{a}_{i}|}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\left|\delta L_{\sum_{i=1}^{j}|\bm{a}_{i}|,\sum_{i=j+1}^{k}|\bm{a}_{i}|}\right|.

This proves the result.
∎

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
\{\bm{a}_{1},\cdots,\bm{a}_{k}\},\{\bm{b}_{1},\cdots,\bm{b}_{k}\}\subset{W}\setminus\{*\}
such that
\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k},\bm{b}_{1}\cdots\bm{b}_{k}\in{W}
. It holds

|L(\bm{a}_{1}\cdots\bm{a}_{k})-L(\bm{b}_{1}\cdots\bm{b}_{k})|\leq 2(k-1)\|\delta L\|+\lvert\sum L(\bm{a}_{i})-\sum L(\bm{b}_{i})\rvert.

We turn our attention to quasimorphisms defined on
\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. For
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
its cyclic permutation group is denoted by
\mathcal{C}_{\bm{a}}
(
\Rightarrow|\mathcal{C}_{\bm{a}}|=|\bm{a}|
). Define

(4.5)

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\bm{a})=\frac{1}{|\bm{a}|}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\bm{a}}}L(\pi(\bm{a})).

We say that
L
is cyclic if
\forall\bm{a}
periodic,
L_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\bm{a})=L(\bm{a})
.

Remark 4.12
.

From the definition of quasimorphism we get that for
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\bm{a}}
then
|L(\pi(\bm{a}))-L(\bm{a})|\leq 2\lVert\delta L\rVert
, and thus
|L_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\bm{a})-L(\bm{a})|\leq 2\lVert\delta L\rVert
. Note also that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

|L_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\bm{a}^{m})-mL_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\bm{a})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert.

For
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\cup\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
define

(4.6)

\displaystyle\overline{L}(\bm{a})\coloneqq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}L(\widetilde{\bm{a}}^{n})=\inf_{n}\frac{L(\widetilde{\bm{a}}^{n})+\lVert\delta L\rVert}{n}.

The limit exists since
(L(\widetilde{\bm{a}}^{n})+\lVert\delta L\rVert)_{n}
is a sub-additive sequence.

Definition 4.13
.

A quasimorphism
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\cup\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
is called homogeneous if for all
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),n\geq 1
it holds
L(\bm{a}^{n})=nL(\bm{a})
.

The set of homogeneous quasimorphisms on
\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
is denoted by
\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
,
\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
, respectively.

Lemma 4.14
.

The function
\overline{L}:{W}\to\mathbb{R}
is a homogeneous quasimorphism. Moreover,

•

\lVert\overline{L}\rVert\leq 32\lVert L\rVert
;

•

\lVert(L-\overline{L})|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq 2\lVert L\rVert
.

It follows that
\overline{L}\sim L
, and is the unique homogeneous quasimorphism in that cohomology class.

Let
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{W},\bm{u},\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v},\bm{v}^{\prime}\in{W}_{M}
such that
\widetilde{\bm{a}}=\bm{a}\bm{u},\widetilde{\bm{b}}=\bm{b}\bm{v},\bm{c}=\widetilde{\bm{a}}\star\widetilde{\bm{b}}=\bm{a}\bm{u}^{\prime}\bm{b}\bm{v}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. By definition of quasimorphism,

\displaystyle|L\left(\widetilde{\bm{a}}\star\widetilde{\bm{b}}\right)-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})|\leq 4\lVert L\rVert

\displaystyle|L\left((\widetilde{\bm{a}}\star\widetilde{\bm{b}})^{2}\right)-L((\widetilde{\bm{a}})^{2})-L((\widetilde{\bm{b}})^{2})|\leq 16\lVert L\rVert.

Arguing by induction we get

\displaystyle|L\left((\bm{c})^{n}\right)-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})^{n})-L((\widetilde{\bm{b}})^{n})|=|L(\bm{a}\bm{u}^{\prime}(\bm{b}\bm{v}^{\prime}\bm{a}\bm{u}^{\prime})^{n-1}\bm{b}\bm{v}^{\prime})-L(\bm{a}\bm{u}(\bm{a}\bm{u})^{n-1})-L(\bm{b}\bm{v}(\bm{b}\bm{v})^{n-1})|

\displaystyle\leq 16\lVert L\rVert+|L((\bm{b}\bm{v}^{\prime}\bm{a}\bm{u}^{\prime})^{n-1})-L((\widetilde{\bm{a}})^{n-1})-L((\widetilde{\bm{b}})^{n-1})|\leq 16\lVert L\rVert+16(n-1)\lVert L\rVert=16n\lVert L\rVert.

Hence
\overline{L}:{W}\to\mathbb{R}
is a quasimorphism with
\lVert\delta\overline{L}\rVert\leq 16\lVert L\rVert
, and by the same token,
\lVert\overline{L}\rVert\leq 32\lVert L\rVert
. Define
L^{\prime}:\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathbb{R}
,
L^{\prime}(\bm{a})=L(\bm{a})+\lVert\delta L\rVert
. Then
\overline{L^{\prime}}=\overline{L}
and
\overline{L^{\prime}}(\bm{a})\leq L^{\prime}(\bm{a})+\lVert\delta L\rVert=L(\bm{a})+2\lVert\delta L\rVert
. Applying the same
-L
, and noting that
\overline{-L}=-\overline{L}
we get

\lVert(L-\overline{L})|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq 2\lVert L\rVert.

Uniqueness is obvious.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

Notice that
\overline{L_{\mathrm{cyc}}}=\overline{L}
, and in particular
\overline{L}
is cyclic.

One sees directly that
\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
are Banach isomorphic.

Corollary 4.16
.

The space
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
is Banach isomorphic to
\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
.

This is completely analogous to
Corollary

3.6
, using
Proposition

4.9
.
∎

4.2.
Quasicocycles

When dealing with quasimorphisms
L:\Gamma\to\mathbb{R}
defined on groups the basic problem concerning bounded cohomology is to determine whether or not
L
is at bounded distance from a zero defect quasimorphism (i.e. a group morphism). To translate the same question to quasimorphisms defined on a SFT we have to face the difficulty that the zero defect quasimorphism will typically be ill-defined, or trivial. To get around this, we will use that quasimorphisms can be used to induce a sequence of functions
(L^{(n)}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R})_{n\geq 0}
with certain properties, and translate the corresponding cohomological problems into this setting.

Definition 4.17
.

For
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and
n\geq 0
we define
L^{(n)}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
by
L^{(n)}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=L(x_{0}\cdots x_{n-1})
. The family of continuous functions
(L^{(n)})_{n\geq 0}
is the quasicocycle associated to
L
.

Given a a sequence of bounded Borel functions
\bm{B}=(B_{n}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R})_{n\geq 1}
we denote by

(4.7)

\displaystyle|\delta\bm{B}_{n,m}|=\sup_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}|B_{n+m}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-B_{m}(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|

(4.8)

\displaystyle\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert=\sup_{n,m}|\delta\bm{B}_{n,m}|

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=\sup_{n\geq 1}\var_{n}(B_{n})

where for a function
B:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
we write
\var_{n}(B)=\sup\{|B(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-B(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})|:[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}=[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}]_{n}\}
.

Definition 4.18
.

A (Bowen) quasicocycle is a sequence
\bm{B}=(B_{n})_{n\geq 1}
of bounded Borel functions satisfying
\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert+\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}<\infty
. The set of all Bowen quasicocycles is denoted by
\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
.

The quasicocycle is continuous if it consists of continuous functions, and is locally constant if
[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}=[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}]_{n}
implies
B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
.

We denote
\mathrm{QCB}^{\,c}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma),\mathrm{QCB}^{\,l}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
the subspaces of continuous and locally constant quasicocycles, respectively.

In the literature sequences satisfying
\lVert\delta\bm{b}\rVert<\infty
are also called almost additive sequences. Note that
\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert=0
if and only if
B_{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}B_{1}\circ\tau
, and
\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=0
if and only if
B_{n}
is locally constant. Denote

\displaystyle\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\bm{B}\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert+\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}+\lVert B_{1}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}.

It follows that
\left(\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma),\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\cdot\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}\right)
is a Banach space.

We remark the following consequence of
[
48
]
.

Theorem 4.19
.

If
\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}^{\,c}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
then there exists
\phi:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
continuous so that

\lim_{n}\frac{1}{n}\lVert B_{n}-S_{n}\phi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}{\infty}=0.

The previous theorem however does not guarantee that
(S_{n}\phi)_{n}
has also finite norm (i.e. that
\phi
has the Bowen property).

Next we address cohomology of quasicocycles.

Definition 4.20
.

The quasicocycle
\bm{B}
is said to be cohomologically trivial if
\sup_{n}\lVert B_{n}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}<\infty
. Two quasicocycles
\bm{B},\bm{B}^{\prime}
are cohomologous (
\bm{B}\sim\bm{B}^{\prime}
) if their difference is cohomologically trivial.

We denote
[\bm{B}]:=\{\bm{B}^{\prime}:\bm{B}^{\prime}\sim\bm{B}\}
the cohomology class of
\bm{B}
, and write

\widetilde{\mathrm{QCB}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)=\left\{[\bm{B}]:\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)\right\}.

The vector space
\widetilde{\mathrm{QCB}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
is equipped with the (complete) norm

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert[\bm{B}]\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=\inf\{\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\bm{B}^{\prime}\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}:\bm{B}\sim\bm{B}^{\prime}\}.

Proposition 4.21
.

The map
\Gamma:\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\widetilde{\mathrm{QCB}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
defined by
\Gamma([L])=[(L^{(n)})_{n}]
is a continuous linear isomorphism, with
\frac{1}{M}\leq\lVert\Gamma\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\mathrm{OP}}\leq 1
.

As a consequence, the spaces
\widetilde{\mathrm{QCB}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma),\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}),\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
are Banach isomorphic.

It is direct to check that
\Gamma
is an injective linear map, and since for every
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})

it holds that
\lVert(L^{(n)})_{n}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=0,\lVert\delta(L^{(n)})_{n}\rVert\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert,\lVert L^{(1)}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}=\lVert L|_{{W}_{1}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}
, we deduce that
\lVert\Gamma\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\mathrm{OP}}\leq 1
. It remains to show that
\Gamma
is surjective and compute the norm of its inverse.

Let
\bm{B}
be a given quasicocycle. For each
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n}
choose a point
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}}\in[\bm{a}]
and define

L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a})=B_{|\bm{a}|}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}}).

If
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},\bm{b}\in{W}_{m}
are concatenable words, then

\displaystyle\begin{cases}L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a}\bm{b})=B_{n+m}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}\bm{b}})\Rightarrow\left|L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a}\bm{b})-B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}\bm{b}})+B_{m}(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}\bm{b}})\right|\leq|\delta\bm{B}_{n,m}|\\
|B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}\bm{b}})-L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a})|,\ |B_{m}(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{\bm{a}\bm{b}})-L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{b})|\leq\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}\end{cases}\Rightarrow

\left|L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a}\bm{b})-L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{a})-L^{\bm{B}}(\bm{b})\right|\leq 2\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}+\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert
and
L^{\bm{B}}
is a quasimorphism. Moreover
\Gamma([L])=[\bm{B}]
. Since
\lVert L^{\bm{B}}|_{{W}_{\leq M}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq\lVert B_{M}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}{\infty}\leq M\lVert B_{1}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}
, it follows that

\lVert L^{\bm{B}}\rVert\leq M\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\bm{B}\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}\Rightarrow\lVert\Gamma^{-1}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\mathrm{OP}}\leq M,

and this proves the first part. The second is direct from
Proposition

4.9
.
∎

Thus we see that the cohomology class of any quasimorphism on a SFT is uniquely determined by a zero defect quasicocycle. Compared to the group case, this may seem surprising at first sight. However, a closer look reveals that the non-triviality of these cohomology classes is due to the fact that they are represented by locally constant functions (which necessarily carry some choices in their construction). At this point we have reached the point where we need further technology to understand the space
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. For this, we introduce a coarse version of Livsic cohomology for dynamical systems, and show that both theories are isomorphic.

5.
Livšic cohomology on SFTs

We start with an extension of a concept introduced by Bowen
[
13
]
. Throughout this section,
\Sigma
denotes a fixed SFT.

Definition 5.1
.

A bounded function
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\subset\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
is said to be a weak Bowen function if

(1)

D_{\varphi}
is dense and
\tau
-invariant.

(2)

There is
C>0
such that if
n\geq 1
and
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in D_{\varphi}
then

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}\Rightarrow\left|S_{n}\varphi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-S_{n}\varphi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\right|\leq C.

We denote by
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=\inf C
, and call it the Bowen constant of
\varphi
.

If, in addition,

3

D_{\varphi}
contains a full measure set for some
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
of full support, we say that
\varphi
is a
\mu
-weak Bowen function. In this case the Bowen norm of
\varphi
is defined as
\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\varphi\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}=\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}+\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}
.

4

D_{\varphi}=\Sigma
then we say that
\varphi
has the Bowen property.

Notation
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Bow}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)(\text{ resp. }\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma))
the set of weak (
\mu-
weak) Bowen functions on
\Sigma
.

Usually, the literature deals with Bowen functions which are furthermore continuous. Let us justify introducing the weaker definition here by stating two theorems (to be proven in the next section) which will be used to establish
Theorem

A
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
R\in\Mat_{d}(\{0,1\})
be an aperiodic and irreducible matrix and consider
\Sigma
the SFT that it determines. Given
\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
and
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, there exist
E=E_{\bm{B}}>0
and
\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu}\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu\right)
defined on a full measure set
\Sigma_{0}(\mu)
so that

(\forall\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma_{0}(\mu),n\geq 1):\ |B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-S_{n}\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|<E.

Corollary 5.3
.

In the same hypotheses as the previous theorem, if
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
has full support, then
\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu}
is a
\mu-
weak Bowen function.

In this part we will look at how unique the function constructed in the previous theorem is: this will lead us to develop some version of Livšic cohomology
[
17
]
for
L^{\infty}-
functions. Complementary to the above, we also have.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\Sigma
be as above. If
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}
is a weak Bowen function then there exists a canonically defined measure
\mu_{\varphi}\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
such that
D_{\varphi}^{c}
is a
\mu_{\varphi}
-null set. In particular,
\mu_{\varphi}
has full support.

Moreover, this measure is ergodic.

This shows that there is essentially no difference between weak Bowen functions and locally constant quasicocycles. For if
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}
is a weak Bowen function, then we can use the measure
\mu_{\varphi}
to average over cylinders the sequence
(S_{n}\varphi)_{n}
and obtain a locally constant quasicocycle associated with
\varphi
. Since this procedure (conditioning with respect to some increasing filtration of
\sigma-
algebras) will be used repeatedly, we recall its construction below.

Conditional expectation

We consider the partitions
\xi^{n}
defined by the Bowen balls of length
n+1
together with the associated filtration of
\sigma-
algebras in
{B}(\Sigma)
,

{B}^{n}=\begin{cases}\{\emptyset,\Sigma\}&n=-1\\
\sigma-\text{algebra generated by }\xi^{n}&n\geq 0.\end{cases}

We write
\xi=\xi^{0}=\{[a]:a\in{A}\}
, and hence
\xi^{n}=\bigvee_{k=0}^{n-1}\tau^{-k}\xi
. It follows that for any probability measure
\mu\in{P}(\Sigma)
,

\mathbb{E}_{#1}[f\nonscript\;|\nonscript\;\mathopen{}\xi^{n}]=\mathbb{E}_{#1}[f\nonscript\;|\nonscript\;\mathopen{}{B}^{n}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\xi^{n}\\
\mu(A)\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\mu(A)}\int_{A}f\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\cdot\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}

where
\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}
is the characteristic function of
A
.

Consider
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}
with the weak Bowen property, and let
\mu_{\varphi}
be the
\tau
-invariant measure associated to it given in
Theorem

5.4
. Define
\bm{B}^{\varphi}=(B_{n}^{\varphi}):\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
with

(5.1)

\displaystyle B_{n}^{\varphi}=\mathbb{E}_{#1}[S_{n}\varphi\nonscript\;|\nonscript\;\mathopen{}\xi^{n}].

Lemma 5.5
.

It holds that
\bm{B}^{\varphi}\in\mathrm{QCB}^{\,l}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
, and furthermore
\lVert\delta\bm{B}^{\varphi}\rVert\leq 6\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}
.

By construction
\bm{B}^{\varphi}
consists of locally constant functions. If
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in[\bm{a}]\cap D_{\varphi},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in[\bm{a}]
, then
|B_{n}^{\varphi}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-S_{n}\varphi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|\leq 2\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}
. From here the bound on the norm follows directly, and thus
\bm{B}^{\varphi}\in\mathrm{QCB}^{\,l}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
.
∎

There is a natural notion of cohomology associated with weak Bowen functions which, in case of functions with the Bowen property, coincides with that given by the Livšic theorem, i.e., two functions with the Bowen property are cohomologous in the Livšic sense if and only if they have the same integral with respect to any
\tau
-invariant measure. To explain this we first define the integral for this more general class of functions.

Integration

We define integrals of quasicocycles with respect to invariant measures. If
\bm{B}=(B_{n})_{n}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
and
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, we define

\mu(\bm{B}):=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\int B_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu.

Existence of this limit follows from sub-additivity of the real valued sequence
(\int B_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu)_{n}
.

Example 5.6
.

Let
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma))
and construct an associated quasicocycle
\bm{B}^{L}=(L^{(n)})_{n}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
by proceeding first as in
Proposition

4.9
, and then considering the associated quasicocycle. If
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
then

\mu(L)\coloneqq\mu(\bm{B}^{L})=\lim_{n}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\bm{a}\in{W}_{n}}\mu([\bm{a}])L(\bm{a}).

It is worth noticing that
\mu(L)
does not depend on the choices made to extend
L
to a fully defined quasimorphism, and likewise, it is invariant in the cohomology class
[L]\in\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma))
.

Proposition 5.7
.

If
\bm{B}=(B_{n})_{n}\in\mathrm{QCB}^{\,c}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
then the map
{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)\ni\mu\to\mu(\bm{B})
is weakly continuous. As a consequence, if
L
is a quasimorphism of a mixing SFT then
{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)\ni\mu\to\mu(L)
is weakly continuous.

The first part follows from
Theorem

4.19
: if
\phi\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
is so that
\lim_{n}\frac{1}{n}\lVert B_{n}-S_{n}\phi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}=0
, then for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
it holds
\mu(\bm{B})=\int\phi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu
.

The second part follows by observing that the quasicocycle associated to a quasimorphism consists of continuous (locally constant) functions.
∎

Periodic points

Let us investigate the behavior at periodic points. For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)
, let
\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta_{\tau^{j}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
be the invariant probability measure supported on the orbit of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
. Each periodic point
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
is associated to a periodic word
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
(
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a})
), thus we write
\mu_{\bm{a}}=\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)
and
m>0
then

\left|\frac{B_{mN}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}{m}-B_{N}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\right|\leq\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert.

The proof is straightforward.

Lemma 5.9
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\tau)
then

\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\bm{B})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}{n}.

By definition,

\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\bm{B})=\frac{1}{N}\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{n}B_{n}(\tau^{j}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}).

For
j
fixed the previous lemma implies that
(\frac{1}{n}B_{n}(\tau^{j}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))_{n}
is convergent, and since
|B_{n+j}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-B_{j}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-B_{n}(\tau^{j}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|\leq\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert
we get that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}B_{n}(\tau^{j}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Example 5.10
.

Let
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
and let
\bm{B}^{L}
be an associated quasicocycle as in the previous example. For
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
,
n\geq 1
we have
L^{nN}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))=L(\bm{a}^{n})
, we deduce

\mu_{\bm{a}}(L)=\lim_{n}\frac{1}{n}L(\bm{a}^{n})=\overline{L}(\bm{a}).

In particular, if
L\in\mathrm{HQM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
,
\mu_{\bm{a}}(L)=L(\bm{a})
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}
be a weak Bowen function. Then for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
such that
D_{\varphi}
contains a full
\mu
-set it holds
\int\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\mu(\bm{B}^{\varphi})
.

For each
n\geq 1
one has

\int\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\frac{1}{n}\int S_{n}\varphi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\frac{1}{n}\int B_{n}^{\varphi}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{n},

where
|\varepsilon_{n}|\leq 2\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}
. Letting
n\to\infty
gives the result.
∎

Notation
.

Due to the above, if
\varphi:D_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}
is a weak Bowen function and
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, we write
\mu(\varphi)=\mu(\bm{B}^{\varphi})
.

5.1.
A Livšic type theorem for quasicocycles.

Observe the following simple but important fact.

Lemma 5.12
.

If
\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
is cohomologically trivial then for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
it holds
\mu(\bm{B})=0
.

In this part we establish the converse.

Example 5.13
.

Let
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma))
be a quasimorphism defined on a mixing SFT, and consider the potential
\varphi_{L}:\Sigma_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
associated to
L
as given in
Theorem

5.4
. Then
\bm{B}^{\varphi_{L}}\in\mathrm{QCB}^{\,l}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
is cohomologous to
\bm{B}^{L}
. It follows directly from
Proposition

5.11
and the previous lemma that
\int\varphi_{L}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varphi_{L}}=\mu_{\varphi_{L}}(\bm{B}^{L})
.

Let us spell some basic considerations.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in[\bm{a}],\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. Then

\left|B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-n\cdot\mu_{\bm{a}}(\bm{B})\right|\leq\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\bm{B}\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}.

Direct computation:

|B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))-B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|\leq\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}

and

\left|n\mu_{\bm{a}}(\bm{B})-B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))\right|=\left|n\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{B_{mn}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))}{mn}-B_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a}))\right|\leq\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert.

∎

Corollary 5.15
.

If
\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
and
\mu(\bm{B})=0
for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, then

\sup_{n}\lVert B_{n}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq\lVert\bm{B}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}+\lVert\delta\bm{B}\rVert.

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathrm{Bow}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
be such that
\mu(\bm{B}^{\varphi})=0
for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
. Then, for every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
on its domain

|S_{n}\varphi(x)|\leq 6\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}.

Use
Lemma

5.5
.
∎

After these preparations we can make a meaningful remark.

Proposition 5.17

(Livšic theorem for quasicocycles)
.

Let
\bm{B},\bm{B}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
. Then
\bm{B}\sim\bm{B}^{\prime}
if and only if for every
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
it holds
\mu_{\bm{a}}(\bm{B})=\mu_{\bm{a}}(\bm{B}^{\prime})
.

The necessary condition is spelled in
Lemma

5.12
, while the sufficient condition follows directly from
Corollary

5.15
.
∎

Corollary 5.18

(Livšic theorem for quasimorphisms)
.

Let
\Sigma
be a mixing SFT and
L,L^{\prime}\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
. Then
L\sim L^{\prime}
if and only if for every
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
it holds
\overline{L}(\bm{a})=\overline{L}^{\prime}(\bm{a})
. It follows that
L\sim L^{\prime}
if and only if
L|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}\sim L^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}
.

It is no loss of generality to assume that
L,L^{\prime}
are homogeneous; the statement of the corollary is direct from the previous proposition and the considerations given in
Example

5.10
.
∎

A general Livšic theorem

Next we deal with cohomology for weak Bowen functions. The central theorem that we will establish is the following.

Theorem 5.19

(Livšic’s Theorem for weak Bowen functions)
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
for some
\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, and assume that for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
it holds
\mu(\varphi)=0
. Then there exists
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\nu\right)
such that

u-u\circ\tau=\varphi\qquad\nu\text{-a.e.}

Moreover,
\lVert u\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{\infty}\!\left(\nu\right)}\leq 6\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}
.

Notation
.

The Koopman operator induced by
\tau
with respect to some invariant measure
\nu
is
T:L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)\toit
,
T\phi=\phi\circ\tau
. We write
T^{*}
for its adjoint.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma),\phi\in L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
and define

u_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}S_{k}\phi

Assume that

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\lVert u_{N}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}<\infty,

(this is true for example if
\sup_{k}\lVert S_{k}\phi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}<\infty
). Then the sequence
(u_{N})_{N}
converges both in
L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
and
\nu\text{-a.e.}
to some function
u\in L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
which is orthogonal to the invariant
\sigma
-algebra of
\tau
. That is, for every invariant set
A
, it holds true
\int_{A}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu=0
. Moreover

(1)

\phi=u-Tu
.

(2)

u-\frac{1}{N}S_{N}u=u_{N}.

Take
u
to be a weak accumulation point of
(u_{N})_{N}
in
L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
: for some sub-sequence
(u_{N_{i}})_{i}
and for every
w\in L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
, it holds
\langle u_{N_{i}},w\rangle\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{}\langle u,w\rangle
. Notice that if
A
is invariant,
\langle u_{N},\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}k\langle\phi,\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}\rangle=\frac{N+1}{2}\langle\phi,\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}\rangle
.

To satisfy the convergence condition, necessarily
\langle\phi,\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}\rangle=0
for every invariant set, hence due to the von Neumann theorem,

\frac{1}{N}\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi\circ\tau^{k}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}\xrightarrow[N\mapsto\infty]{}0.

Now,

\displaystyle u_{N}-u_{N}\circ\tau=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}(S_{k}\phi-S_{k}\phi\circ\tau)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}(\phi-\phi\circ\tau^{k})=\phi-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi\circ\tau^{k},

therefore for
w\in L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\langle u_{N_{i}},w\rangle-\langle u_{N_{i}}\circ\tau,w\rangle=\lim_{i\to\infty}\langle u_{N_{i}},w\rangle-\langle u_{N_{i}},T^{*}w\rangle=\langle u-u\circ\tau,w\rangle

and

\displaystyle\langle u-u\circ\tau,w\rangle=\lim_{i\to\infty}\langle\phi-\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{i}}\phi\circ\tau^{k},w\rangle=\langle\phi,w\rangle.

This gives

\langle u-u\circ\tau-\phi,w\rangle=0\quad\forall w\in L^{2}\!\left(\nu\right)

which shows that any accumulation point
u
of
(u_{N})_{N}
solves the cohomological equation for
\phi
.

Next, for
u
as above we have
u-u\circ\tau^{k}=S_{k}\phi
and hence summing over
k=1,\ldots,N
we get that

u-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}u\circ\tau^{k}=u_{N}\Rightarrow u-u_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}u\circ\tau^{k}.

Since we showed that
\int_{A}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu=0
for every invariant set
A
, the Birkhoff and von Neumann ergodic theorems applied to
u
give the pointwise and
L^{2}-
convergence.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

In the previous lemma, and assuming
\sup_{k}\lVert S_{k}\phi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}<\infty
, it is classical to show that the cohomological equation has a solution in
L^{2}
: consider
\mathfrak{X}
the set of convex combinations of
(S_{k}\varphi)
and note that its weak
L^{2}
closure is compact, and of course convex. The map
u\in\overline{\mathfrak{X}}\to\phi+u\circ T
preserves
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}
and is continuous, so by the Schauder-Tychonoff theorem, there exists
u\in\overline{\mathfrak{X}}
satisfying
u=\phi+u\circ T
, hence
u
satisfies the cohomological equation in
L^{2}
. It follows that there exists
(v_{n})_{n}\subset\mathfrak{X}
such that
\lVert u-v_{n}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}\xrightarrow[n\mapsto\infty]{}0
. Note however that this argument does not give the pointwise convergence statements, which will allow us to improve the regularity of
u
.

Corollary 5.22
.

Under the same hypotheses as in the previous theorem, if

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\lVert u_{N}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{\infty}}<\infty,

(for example, if
\sup_{k}\lVert S_{k}\phi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{\infty}}<\infty
), then there exists
u\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
with
\lVert u\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{\infty}}\leq\liminf_{N}\lVert u_{N}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{\infty}}
so that
\lim_{N}u_{N}=u
both
\nu\text{-a.e.}
and in
L^{\infty}
. Moreover,
\phi=u-u\circ\tau
.

Since
\liminf_{N\to\infty}\lVert u_{N}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!L^{2}}<\infty
, we can apply the previous theorem and deduce
\nu\text{-a.e.}
convergence
u_{N}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}u
. The dominated convergence theorem implies the rest.
∎

We now are ready to prove
Theorem

5.19
.

We can assume that the domain of
\varphi
is a measurable, invariant
\nu-
full measure set. Let
u_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}S_{k}\varphi
. By hypotheses and
Corollary

5.16
we get that
|u_{N}(x)|\leq 6\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!B}
, for every
x\in D_{\varphi}
and
N\geq 1
, therefore the result follows from
Corollary

5.22
.
∎

Definition 5.23
.

Let
\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
. A function
\phi\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\nu\right)
is a
L^{\infty}
-coboundary if
\phi=u-u\circ\tau
for some
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\nu\right)
. The space of coboundaries in
L^{\infty}\!\left(\nu\right)
is denoted by
\mathrm{Cob}^{\,\infty}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
.

Note that
\mathrm{Cob}^{\,\infty}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)\subset\left(\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma),\lvert\!\lvert\!\lvert\cdot\rvert\!\rvert\!\rvert\right)
is a closed subspace. We equip

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)/\thicksim\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)}{\mathrm{Cob}^{\,\infty}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)}

with the induced norm.

Theorem 5.24
.

Let
\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
be a fully supported measure. Then there exists a Banach isomorphism between
\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and
\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)/\thicksim
.

Given
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
consider its associated quasicocycle
\bm{B}^{L}
, and the corresponding associated potential
\varphi_{L}:\Sigma_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
as given in
Theorem

5.2
. Using
Proposition

4.21
and
Lemma

5.5
we get that
\bm{B}^{\varphi_{L}}\sim\bm{B}^{L}
, and since
\bm{B}^{L}
is cohomologically trivial if and only if
L
is cohomologically trivial in
\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, we deduce that
L
is cohomologically trivial if and only if
\bm{B}^{\varphi_{L}}
is uniformly bounded. Due to
Theorem

5.19
, this happens if and only if
\varphi_{L}=u-u\circ\tau
, for some
u\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
.

The above shows that the linear map
\Gamma:\widetilde{\mathrm{QM}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)/\thicksim,\Gamma([L])=[\varphi_{L}]
is bijective, and it is direct to check that it is continuous, hence a Banach isomorphism.
∎

5.2.
Livšic cohomology for suspension flows

Bowen functions in the natural extension

Consider
\widetilde{\Sigma}
, a two-sided SFT. The notion of weak Bowen (
\mu-
weak Bowen function) is exactly the same in this context as in the one-sided shift. Note that the proof of
Theorem

5.19
applies to this type of function without modifications.

Let
\pi:\widetilde{\Sigma}\to\Sigma
be the projection onto the non-negative coordinates,
\pi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}=(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
: this map is continuous, surjective and verifies
\pi\circ\tau=\tau\circ\pi
(that is, it is a semi-conjugacy between
\tau:\widetilde{\Sigma}\toit
and
\tau:\Sigma\toit
). Moreover, it induces an isomorphism
\pi_{\#}:{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})\to{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
which can be characterized as follows (cf. page
28
in
[
43
]
). Fix
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
. Given
f\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
, it induces naturally an element
\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
by
\widetilde{f}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=f(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+})
. Define, for
n\geq 0
,

\widetilde{\mu}(\widetilde{f}\circ\tau^{-n})\coloneqq\mu(f).

This determines a bounded positive linear functional
\widetilde{\mu}
on the dense subspace
\{\widetilde{f}\circ\tau^{-n}:n\geq 0,f\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)\}\subset\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
, hence it extends uniquely to a bounded linear functional
\widetilde{\mu}:\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})\to\mathbb{R}
with
\lVert\widetilde{\mu}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!OP}=\lVert\mu\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!OP}=\mu(1_{\Sigma})=1
, therefore it is a probability on
\widetilde{\Sigma}
. Furthermore,
\mu
is ergodic if and only if
\widetilde{\mu}
is ergodic.

Notation
.

If
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
,
\widetilde{\mu}
denotes its unique lift to
{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
.

Proposition 5.25
.

If
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
is of full support, then the spaces
\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)/\thicksim
and
\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\mu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})/\thicksim
are Banach isomorphic.

Consider the linear continuous mappings

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}:\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\mu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})\ni\phi\mapsto\left(\bm{B}=(\mathbb{E}_{#1}[S_{n}\phi\nonscript\;|\nonscript\;\mathopen{}\xi^{n}])_{n}\right)\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)\mapsto\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu}\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}:\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)\ni\phi\to\phi\circ\pi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\mu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma}).

By definition we have that
\widetilde{\mu}(\phi)=\widetilde{\mu}(\bm{B})
, and since
\bm{B}
is constant on
\xi^{n}
,
\widetilde{\mu}(\bm{B})=\mu(\bm{B})
. Now
\bm{B}\sim(S_{n}\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu})_{n}
, and thus
\widetilde{\mu}(\phi)=\mu(\varphi_{\bm{B},\mu})
. From this and
Theorem

5.19
one gets that
\Gamma_{1}
induces a continuous injective linear map
\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\mu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})/\thicksim\to\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)/\thicksim
. Using
\Gamma_{2}
it follows that this map is surjective as well, hence the result.
∎

Remark 5.26
.

It is a classical result of Sinai that any Hölder function on
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is continuously cohomologous to a Hölder function on
\Sigma
. On the other hand, even for well behaved measures (as the entropy maximizing measure of
\tau
) there are
L^{\infty}-
coboundaries that are not continuously cohomologous to any function depending only on the non-negative coordinates, thus, not continuously cohomologous to any Hölder function
[
49
]
. In particular, if
\phi
is such a function, then
\phi
is not continuously cohomologous to
\Gamma_{1}(\phi)\circ\pi
.
This shows the necessity of working with these broader cohomology theories instead of the classical versions using continuous functions.

Flows

Consider
\mathfrak{t}=(\tau_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}:\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f}\toit
a suspension flow as in
Theorem

2.16
, and for
\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
let
\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}\in{P}_{\mathfrak{t}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f})
be the unique invariant measure that it determines (see
Equation

2.4
). Since
\mathfrak{t}
is a metric Anosov flow, one can define
\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}-
Bowen functions and their Livšic cohomology in the same way as in
Definitions

2.7
and
2.9
.

It is convenient to define
P:\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f}\toit
the return map to the section
S=\{[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0]:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}
,

P([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])=[\tau(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}),0].

Note that
P
preserves
\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}
. If
\psi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma_{f})
define

(5.3)

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])=\int_{0}^{f(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-t}\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},s])\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s

(5.4)

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\hat{\psi}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0])=\int_{0}^{f(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},s])\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

Lemma 5.27
.

Let
\psi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma_{f})
. Then
\psi
is a Livšic coboundary with respect to
\mathfrak{t}
if and only if
\hat{\psi}
is a Livšic coboundary for the return map
P
.

The proof is standard, and is essentially an application of the fundamental theorem of Calculus.

On the other hand, if
\hat{\psi}
is a Livšic coboundary with respect to the dynamics
\tau
,
\hat{\psi}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\tau x)
let

U([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])=u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-\int_{0}^{t}\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},s])\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

U\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\widetilde{\nu}\right)
and by direct computation
U([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])-U(P[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])=\hat{\psi}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},t])
, which shows that
\hat{\psi}
is a coboundary with respect to
P
, hence
\psi
is a Livšic coboundary for the flow
\mathfrak{t}
.

If
\alpha
is a periodic orbit of
\mathfrak{t}
we denote, as in the case of the geodesic flow,

\av_{\alpha}(\phi)=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{S_{T}\phi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0])}{T}

where
[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0]\in\alpha
: of course,
\av_{\alpha}(\phi)
is just the integral of
\phi
with respect to the periodic measure supported on
\alpha
. We remark the following simple fact.

Lemma 5.28
.

There exists a bijection between the periodic orbits of
\mathfrak{t}
and the periodic orbits of
\tau:\Sigma\toit
.

In view of the above it makes sense to consider
\av_{\bm{a}}
for
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})

Corollary 5.29
.

The map
\Gamma:\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma_{f})\to\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\nu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
given by
\Gamma(\phi)=\hat{\phi}
induces a Banach isomorphism
\Gamma_{*}:\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma_{f})/\thicksim\to\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\widetilde{\nu}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})/\thicksim
.

Moreover,
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f})
are Livšic cohomologous if and only if for every
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
,
\av_{\bm{a}}(\hat{\psi})=\av_{\bm{a}}(\hat{\psi}^{\prime})
.

It is direct to check that
\Gamma
is linear and continuous. Note that given
\varphi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
,
\varphi=\Gamma(\psi)
, where

\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0])=\frac{1}{f(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\varphi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
. Hence by the discussion above induces a bijective linear isomorphism
\Gamma_{*}
between the quotients. Note that for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in[\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n}
,

|S_{n}\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},0])-S_{n}\psi([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},0])|=|S_{n}\hat{\psi}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-S_{n}\hat{\psi}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})|.

From here one deduces that
\Gamma_{*}
is continuous with respect to the corresponding Bowen norms, hence a Banach isomorphism.

The second part is a consequence of
Theorem

5.19
: necessity is clear, while for sufficiency,
\av_{\bm{a}}(\psi)=\av_{\bm{a}}(\psi^{\prime})
implies that
\hat{\psi}\sim\hat{\psi}^{\prime}
, hence
\psi\sim\psi^{\prime}
.
∎

Back to geodesic flows

Finally, let
\mathfrak{g}=(g_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}:{E}\toit
be a geodesic flow corresponding to a closed locally
\mathrm{CAT}\!\left(-a^{2}\right)
space, and consider a suspension flow
\mathfrak{t}=(\tau_{t}):\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f}\toit
as in
Theorem

2.16
; the corresponding semi-conjugacy is
h:\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f}\to{E}
.

Corollary 5.30
.

In the hypotheses above, consider a fully supported measure
\nu_{\mathfrak{g}}\in{P}_{\mathfrak{g}}({E})
. Then
\phi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{g}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({E})
is a Livšic coboundary if and only if for every
\alpha
periodic orbit of
\mathfrak{g}
,
\av_{\alpha}(\phi)=0
.

Consider the isomorphism
h_{*}:\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{t}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f})\to\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\nu_{\mathfrak{g}}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({E})
(
Corollary

2.17
), which preserves cohomology classes. If
\av_{\alpha}(\phi)=0
for every periodic orbit of
\mathfrak{g}
, then
\av_{\alpha^{\prime}}(h^{-1}_{*}\phi)=0
for every periodic orbit
\alpha^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{t}
that lies in some open dense set
D\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{f}
.

This is enough to guarantee that the quasicocycle defined by
h^{-1}_{*}\phi
is uniformly bounded, hence its integral with respect to every invariant measure of
\mathfrak{t}
is zero. Due to
Corollary

5.29
, we deduce that
h^{-1}_{*}\phi
is a Livšic coboundary, hence
\phi
is a Livšic coboundary as well. Necessity is obvious.
∎

The previous Corollary in fact implies
Theorem

E
, where no assumptions on ergodicity are made. This is consequence of the fact that any invariant measure can be written as an integral of ergodic measures (Ergodic Decomposition Theorem). The proof of this theorem can be found for example in
[
50
]
.

6.
Ergodic theory of quasimorphisms

In this section we develop a thermodynamic formalism for quasimorphisms on SFTs (equivalently, quasicocycles), and use it to characterize their cohomology classes by means of certain invariant measures for the shift. These measures are uniquely associated with Livšic cohomology classes of weak Bowen functions (this is the content of
Theorem

5.2
, which we prove here), and we explain how to construct them and analyze their dynamical properties.

6.1.
Pressure for quasimorphisms

Quasi-bounded sequences

Given a sequence of real numbers
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}=(a_{n})_{n}
, denote

(6.1)

\displaystyle\delta_{n,m}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})=a_{n+m}-a_{n}-a_{m}

(6.2)

\displaystyle\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert=\sup_{n,m}|\delta_{n,m}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}|.

Recall that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}
is subadditive if
\forall n,m
,
\delta(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})_{n,m}\leq 0
(i.e.
a_{n+m}\leq a_{n}+a_{m}
). We denote by
\mathcal{S}_{sa}
the set of all sub-additive sequences. If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\in\mathcal{S}_{sa}
, then for every
m=kn+r,0\leq r<n
we have

\frac{a_{m}}{m}-\frac{a_{n}}{n}\leq\frac{1}{r}a_{r}.

It follows that for such a sequence,

\exists\lim_{n}\frac{a_{n}}{n}=\inf\frac{a_{n}}{n}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}.

Corollary 6.1
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\in\mathcal{S}_{sa}
and
\lim_{n}\frac{a_{n}}{n}=0
then
a_{n}\geq 0
for every
n
.

Definition 6.2
.

A sequence
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}
is quasi-bounded if
\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert<\infty
. The set of quasi-bounded sequences is denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{qb}
.

For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\in\mathcal{S}_{qb}
the sequence
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle b}}{\underline{\textstyle b}}{\underline{\scriptstyle b}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle b}}=(b_{n}=a_{n}+\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert)_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle b_{n+m}=a_{n+m}+\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert=\delta_{n,m}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}+a_{n}+a_{m}+\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert\leq b_{n}+b_{m}

and therefore there exists
P(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})=\lim_{n}\frac{a_{n}}{n}
. Note that for any
C\geq\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle(}}{\underline{\textstyle(}}{\underline{\scriptstyle(}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle(}}a)\rVert
,
P(a)=\inf\frac{a_{n}+C}{n}
.

Corollary 6.3
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\in\mathcal{S}_{qb}
then
\sup_{n}|a_{n}-nP(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})|\leq\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert
. In particular, if
P(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})=0
then
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\in\ell^{\infty}
.

Suppose first that
P(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}})=0
. Applying
Corollary

6.1
to
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle b}}{\underline{\textstyle b}}{\underline{\scriptstyle b}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle b}}=(a_{n}+\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert)_{n}
we get
b_{n}\geq 0\Rightarrow a_{n}\geq-\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert
, and by considering

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle c}}{\underline{\textstyle c}}{\underline{\scriptstyle c}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle c}}=(a_{n}-\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert)_{n}
we get
a_{n}\leq\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert
and the claim follows in this case. In general, the sequence
\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}}=(a_{n}-nP(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}))_{n}
satisfies
\lVert\delta\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}}\rVert=\lVert\delta\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle a}}{\underline{\textstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptstyle a}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle a}}\rVert
and
\lim_{n}\frac{\widetilde{a}_{n}}{n}=0
, hence the result.
∎

Fix
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and denote

(6.3)

\displaystyle Z_{n}(L)=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{L(\bm{a})}

(6.4)

\displaystyle P_{n}(L)=\log Z_{n}(L).

Recall that
M\in\mathbb{N}
is so that
R^{k}>0
, for every
k\geq M
, and we remark that given
\bm{a}=a_{0}\cdots a_{n-1},\bm{b}=b_{0}\cdots b_{m-1}\in{W}
the number of words
\bm{c}\in{W}_{k}
such that
\bm{a}\bm{c}\bm{b}\in{W}
is
R^{k+1}_{a_{n-1}b_{0}}
.

Convention
.

Recall that for
\bm{a}\in{W}
we are denoting
\widetilde{\bm{a}}
any periodic word starting with
\bm{a}
of size at most
|\bm{a}|+M
. To avoid carrying constants, given a quasimorphism on
{W}
we re-define
\lVert\delta L\rVert
so that for any
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{W}

\displaystyle|L(\widetilde{\bm{a}}\star\widetilde{\bm{b}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert

and furthermore
\lVert\delta L_{\mathrm{cyc}}\rVert\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert
.

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists some
D>0
so that for every
n,m\geq 3M
it holds

\displaystyle D^{-1}\leq\frac{Z_{n+m}(L)}{Z_{n}(L)\cdot Z_{m}(L)}\leq D.

Step 1:
comparison between
Z_{n}(L)
and
Z_{n+M}(L)
.

For
i,j\in{A}
we fix
\bm{u}^{i,j}\in{W}_{M}
so that
i\bm{u}^{i,j}j\in{W}
. Given
\bm{a}=a_{1}\cdots a_{n}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
we get

Z_{n}(L)\leq e^{\lVert L\rVert}\sum_{\bm{\in}\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{L(\bm{a}\bm{u}^{a_{n},a_{1}})}\leq e^{\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n+M}(L).

Assume that
n>M
: if
\bm{a}\bm{v}=a_{1}\cdots a_{n-M}v_{1}\cdots v_{2M}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, then
\bm{a}\bm{u}^{a_{n-M},a_{1}}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, and
|L(\bm{a}\bm{v})-L(\bm{a}\bm{u}^{a_{n-M},a_{1}})|\leq\lVert L\rVert
. We can thus define a function
\kappa:\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
by

\kappa(\bm{a}\bm{v})=\begin{cases}\bm{a}v_{1}\cdots v_{M}&\text{if }\bm{a}v_{1}\cdots v_{M}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\\
\bm{a}\bm{u}^{a_{n-M},a_{1}}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

By construction
\kappa
is surjective. According to
[
51
]
, there is some constants
C_{1},\htop(\tau)>0
(
\htop(\tau)
is the topological entropy of
\tau
) so that

C_{1}^{-1}e^{M\htop(\tau)}\leq\frac{\#\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}{\#\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}\leq C_{1}e^{M\htop(\tau)},

which implies that
\kappa
is at most
C_{2}=[C_{1}e^{M\htop(T)}+1]
-to-one. Therefore

\displaystyle Z_{n+M}(L)\leq e^{\lVert L\rVert}\sum_{\bm{a}\bm{v}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{L(\kappa(\bm{a}\bm{v}))}\leq C_{2}\cdot e^{\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n}(L).

Step 2:
comparison between
Z_{n+n}(L)
and
Z_{n}(L)\cdot Z_{m}(L)
.

We fix
n,m>M
and argue as above to compute (using the previous equation)

\displaystyle Z_{n}(L)\cdot Z_{m}(L)\leq e^{\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n+m+2M}(L)\Rightarrow Z_{n}(L)\cdot Z_{m}(L)\leq C_{2}^{2}e^{3\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n+m}(L).

For the reverse inequality we use again
[
51
]
and
Lemma

4.3
to construct surjective map
\kappa:\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+m+2M}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\times\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{m}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
, which is
C_{3}
-finite to one, where
C_{3}
does not depend on
n,m
. We thus get

\displaystyle Z_{n+m}(L)\leq e^{2\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n+m+2M}(L)\leq C_{3}e^{3\lVert L\rVert}Z_{n}(L)Z_{m}(L).

Re-arranging the constants we obtain the claimed inequalities.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

For
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
it holds
(P_{n}(L))_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{qb}
.

Definition 6.6
.

The pressure of
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\cup\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))
is

(6.5)

\displaystyle\Ptop(L)=\lim_{n}\frac{P_{n}(L)}{n}

By
Corollary

6.3
it holds

(6.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{n}|P_{n}-n\Ptop(L)|<\infty;

we write

(6.7)

\displaystyle\hat{L}(\bm{a})=L(\bm{a})-P_{|\bm{a}|}(L),\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}).

Naturally,

\displaystyle Z_{n}(\hat{L}):=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{\hat{L}(\bm{a})}=1

and
\Ptop(\hat{L})=0
.

6.2.
The invariant measure associated to a quasimorphism

Recall that if
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
then
\mu_{\bm{a}}\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
denotes the measure supported on the orbit of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a})=\bm{a}\bm{a}\cdots
. We write
\delta_{\bm{a}}=\delta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle p}}{\underline{\textstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptstyle p}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle p}}(\bm{a})}
, so
\mu_{\bm{a}}=\frac{1}{|\bm{a}|}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\bm{a}}}\delta_{\pi\bm{a}}
.

Given a quasimorphism
L
we consider the (
\tau
-invariant) measures

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mu_{L}^{N}=\frac{1}{Z_{N}(L)}\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{L(\bm{a})}\delta_{\bm{a}}=\sum_{\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}e^{\hat{L}(\bm{a})}\delta_{\bm{a}}.

Lemma 6.7
.

There exists
E>0
so that for any
N
sufficiently large and any
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n}
it holds

\displaystyle E^{-1}\leq\frac{\mu_{L}^{n+N}([\bm{a}])}{\exp\left(L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-P_{n}(L)\right)}\leq E.

Let
A=\{\bm{c}=\bm{a}\bm{u}\bm{b}\bm{v}\in\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{n+2M+N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(:)|\bm{u}|=|\bm{v}|=M\}
. It follows that there exists some constant
C>0
which does not depend on
n
, so that
C^{-1}\leq\frac{\#A}{\#\mathrm{Fix}^{\,}_{N}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})}\leq C
. If
\bm{c}\in A
, then
