|L(\bm{c})-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert

and thus

\displaystyle\mu_{L}^{n+N}([\bm{a}])Z_{n+N+2M}(L)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{c}\in A}e^{L(\bm{c})}=e^{L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})}\sum_{\bm{c}\in A}e^{L(\bm{c})-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})}e^{L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})}

is uniformly comparable to
e^{L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})}Z_{N+M}(L)
. By
Lemma

6.4
,

\displaystyle\mu_{L}^{n+N}([\bm{a}])\approx e^{L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})}\frac{Z_{N+M}(L)}{Z_{n+N+2M}(L)}\approx e^{L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-P_{n}(L)}.

∎

This directly implies.

Corollary 6.8
.

For every
n\geq 1
and
N
sufficiently large it holds: for every
\bm{a}\in{W}

E^{-1}\leq\frac{\mu_{L}^{n+M}([\bm{a}])}{\mu_{L}^{n+M}([\widetilde{\bm{a}}])}\leq E.

Corollary 6.9
.

For every
n,m\geq 1
and
N
sufficiently large, it holds: if
\bm{a}\bm{b}\in{W}_{n+m},\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},\bm{b}\in{W}_{m}
then

E^{-1}\leq\frac{\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\bm{a}\bm{b}])}{\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\bm{a}])\cdot\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\bm{b}])}\leq E.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\bm{a}\bm{b}])}{\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\widetilde{\bm{a}}])\cdot\mu_{L}^{N+n+m}([\widetilde{\bm{b}}])}

\displaystyle\approx e^{L(\bm{a}\star\bm{b})-L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-L(\widetilde{\bm{b}})}e^{P_{n+m}(L)-P_{n}(L)-P_{m}(L)}

which is bounded, from above and below independently of
n,m,N
.
∎

Recall that an invariant measure
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
is mixing if for every
A,B\in{B}(\Sigma)
,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mu(A\cap\tau^{-k}B)=\mu(A)\mu(B).

Clearly such a measure is ergodic.

Proposition 6.10
.

The sequence
(\mu_{L}^{N})_{N}
is weakly convergent to some probability
\mu_{L}\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
. Moreover,
\mu_{L}
is mixing, has full support, and there exists some uniform constant
E>0
so that for every pair of concatenable words
\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},\bm{b}\in{W}_{m}
and
k\geq n
, it satisfies

(6.9)

\displaystyle E^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{L}([\bm{a}])}{\exp\left(L(\widetilde{\bm{a}})-n\Ptop(L)\right)}\leq E

(6.10)

\displaystyle E^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{L}([\bm{a}]\cap\tau^{-k}[\bm{b}])}{\mu_{L}([\bm{a}])\cdot\mu_{L}([\bm{b}])}\leq E.

Due to
Corollary

6.3
,
\sup_{n}|P_{n}-n\Ptop(L)|<\infty
, and thus by the corollary above and re-defining
E
if necessary, we deduce that property (
6.9
) is true for any accumulation point
\mu_{L}
of
(\mu_{L}^{N})_{N}
. On the other hand, by
Corollary

6.9
, since
[\bm{a}]\cap[\tau^{-n}\bm{b}]=[\bm{a}\bm{b}]
, we deduce that

\bm{a}\in{W}_{n},\bm{b}\in{W}_{m},\bm{a}\bm{b}\in{W}\Rightarrow E\leq\frac{\mu_{L}([\bm{a}]\cap\tau^{-n}[\bm{b}])}{\mu_{L}([\bm{a}])\cdot\mu_{L}([\bm{b}])}\leq E.

For
l=k-n+1>0
we can write
[\bm{a}\cap\tau^{-k}\bm{b}]=\bigcup_{\bm{c}\in A_{l}}[\bm{a}\bm{c}\bm{b}]
, where

A_{l}={\bm{c}\in{W}_{l}:\bm{a}\bm{c}\bm{b}\in{W}}
: note that the sum
\sum_{\bm{c}}\mu_{L}([\bm{a}\bm{c}])
is uniformly comparable to
\sum_{\bm{d}\in{W}_{l+M}}\mu([\bm{a}\bm{d}])=\mu_{L}([\bm{a}])
, thus

\displaystyle\mu_{L}([\bm{a}\cap\tau^{-n}\bm{b}])

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{c}\in{W}_{l}}\mu_{L}([\bm{a}\bm{c}\bm{b}])\approx\mu_{L}([\bm{a}])\cdot\mu_{L}([\bm{b}]).

Since the algebra of cylinders is generating, a classical measure theory argument implies the following: for every
A,B\in{B}(\Sigma)
,

(1)

\liminf_{k\to\infty}\mu(A\cap\tau^{-k}B)\geq E^{-1}\mu_{L}(A)\cdot\mu_{L}(B)
;

(2)

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\mu(A\cap\tau^{-k}B)\leq E\mu_{L}(A)\cdot\mu_{L}(B)
.

The first condition implies that every power of
\tau
is ergodic: this fact and
2)
tell us that we are in the hypotheses of a theorem of D. Ornstein
[
52
]
that guarantees that
\mu
is mixing.

By (
6.9
) we have any pair of accumulation points of
(\mu_{L}^{N})_{N}
are non-singular with respect to each other and have full support, hence by ergodicity they have to coincide. It follows that there is only one (necessarily mixing) accumulation point of
(\mu_{L}^{N})_{N}
.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

If
L\sim L^{\prime}
then it is direct from the construction that
\mu_{L}=\mu_{L^{\prime}}
.

6.3.
The potential associated to the quasimorphism

Here we will show that to each measure
\mu_{L}
as constructed above, it corresponds a weak Bowen function.

Define for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma,k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\varphi_{L}^{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\log\frac{\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k})}{\mu_{L}([\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-1})}=\log\frac{\mu_{L}([x_{0}\cdots x_{k-1}])}{\mu_{L}([x_{1}\cdots x_{k-1}])}.

Lemma 6.12
.

The sequence
(\varphi_{L}^{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))_{k\geq 1}
is uniformly bounded.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma
. The quotient
\frac{\mu_{L}([x_{0}\cdots x_{k-1}])}{\mu_{L}([x_{1}\cdots x_{k-1}])}
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{L}([x_{0}\cdots x_{k-1}])}{\exp(L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})-n\Ptop(L))}\cdot\frac{\exp(L(\widetilde{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n-1)})-(n-1)\Ptop(L))}{\mu_{L}([x_{1}\cdots x_{k-1}])}

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})-L(\widetilde{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n-1)})+\Ptop(L)\right)

so by (
6.9
),

|\varphi_{L}^{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})|\leq 2E+\lVert\delta L\rVert+\Ptop(L).

∎

Lemma 6.13
.

For every
n\geq 1,k>n
and
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma
,

\left|\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\varphi_{L}^{k-l}(\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})+n\Ptop(L)\right|\leq 2\log E+\Ptop(L)+\lVert\delta L\rVert.

Let us compute

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k})}{\mu_{L}([\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-n})}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k})-\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l})-\sum_{l=1}^{n}\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l})-\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{l+1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\log\left(\frac{\mu_{L}([\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l})}{\mu_{L}([\tau^{l+1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-l-1})}\right)=\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\varphi_{L}^{k-l}(\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

Using
Equation

6.9
we get

\displaystyle|\log\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k})-L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(k)})+k\Ptop(L)|\leq\log E

and

\displaystyle|\log\mu_{L}([\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{k-n})-L(\widetilde{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(k-n)})+(k-n)\Ptop(L)|\leq\log E

hence

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\varphi_{L}^{k-l}(\tau^{l}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(\tilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})+n\Ptop(L)\right|\leq 2\log E+\Ptop(L)+\lVert\delta L\rVert.

∎

We are ready to show the existence of a weak Bowen function associated to
L
.

Theorem 6.14
.

The sequence
(\varphi_{L}^{n})_{n}
converges both
\mu_{L}\text{-a.e.}
and in
L^{p},\forall p\in[1,+\infty)
to some function
\varphi_{L}\in L^{\infty}(\mu_{L})
.

The sequence of continuous functions
(e^{\varphi_{L}^{n}})_{n}
is bounded by
Lemma

6.12
, and one has

e^{\varphi_{L}^{n}}=\sum_{A\in\xi}\mathbb{E}_{#1}[\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}|\tau^{-1}{B}^{n}]\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{A}

By the (increasing) Martingale convergence theorem and Doob’s inequality (see for example
[
53
]
) it follows that this sequence converges almost everywhere and in
L^{p},\forall p\in[1,+\infty)
to some function
e^{\varphi_{L}}\in L^{[}\infty](\mu_{L})
.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

We can argue similarly and guarantee that the set of points
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
where

\exists\lim_{k\to\infty}\log\frac{\mu_{L}([x_{r}\cdots x_{k-1}])}{\mu_{L}([x_{r+1}\cdots x_{k-1}])}

is of full
\mu_{L}
measure for every
r\geq 0
, hence by induction we get that the set
\Sigma_{0}
where
(\varphi_{L}^{n})_{n}
converges is invariant under
\tau
. Replacing
\Sigma_{0}
by
\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\tau^{-n}\Sigma_{0}
it is no loss of generality to assume that
\Sigma_{0}=\tau(\Sigma_{0})=\tau^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})
.

Definition 6.16
.

The function
\varphi_{L}:\Sigma_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
is the potential associated to
L
.

Due to
Lemma

6.13
we have that:

Corollary 6.17
.

For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma_{0},n\in\mathbb{N}
it holds

|S_{n}\varphi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})+n\Ptop(L)-L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})|\leq 2\log E+\log D+\lVert\delta L\rVert.

Therefore
\varphi_{L}
has the weak Bowen property.

6.4.
The equilibrium state associated to the quasicocycle

We are now interested in determining how unique
\mu_{L}
is. Here we establish the following.

Theorem 6.18

(Variational principle)
.

It holds that
\Ptop(L)=\sup_{\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)}\{h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)\}=h_{\mu_{L}}(\tau)+\mu_{L}(L)
. Moreover, the measure
\mu_{L}
is the unique
\tau
-invariant measure where the equality is attained.

We recall that for
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
the number
h_{\mu}(\tau)
is the metric entropy of
\tau
with respect to
\mu
. It can be computed as

h_{\mu}(\tau)=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac{H_{\mu}(\xi^{n})}{n}=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{A\in\xi^{(n)}}\mu(A)\log\mu(A);

this is consequence of the classical Sinai-Kolmogorov theorem (see for example
[
54
]
). If
\mu
is ergodic, then the Brin-Katok formula
[
55
]
permits to compute the entropy also as

h_{\mu}(\tau)=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac{\log\mu([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})}{n}\quad\mu\text{-a.e.}\ \mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}.

The number
P_{\mu}(L)\coloneqq h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)
will be referred to as the metric pressure of the pair
(\mu,L)
.

Since
(\tau,\mu_{L})
is ergodic, using (
6.9
) and Birkhoff’s ergodic theorem we get that for
\mu_{L}\text{-a.e.}\ \mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
,

\displaystyle h_{\mu_{L}}(\tau)=\lim\frac{1}{n}-\log\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})=\lim_{n}\frac{-S_{n}\varphi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}{n}=-\int\varphi_{L}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}=\Ptop(L)-\mu_{L}(L).

Therefore
P_{\mu_{L}}(L)=\Ptop(L)
. We now show that
\mu_{L}
is the unique shift invariant measure satisfying the equality, and that for any other invariant measure
\mu
the metric pressure of
(\mu,L)
is strictly smaller than
\Ptop(L)
.

To verify the remaining parts we modify an argument due to P. Spitzer. For
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
given we consider the Borel functions

M_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):=\begin{cases}\frac{\mu_{L}([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})}{\mu([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})}&\mu([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})\neq 0\\
0&\mu([\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}]_{n})=0.\end{cases}

Then
(M_{n})_{n}
is a martingale relative to
\{{B}^{n}\}_{n}
in
(\Sigma,\mu)
, and converges to either the zero function if
\mu\perp\mu_{L}
, or to
\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu}
if
\mu_{L}\ll\mu
. In the case when
\mu
is singular with respect to
\mu_{L}
, we let
N_{n}=-\log M_{n}
: then
(N_{n})_{n}
is a super-martingale, with

\int N_{n}^{-}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\int(\log M_{n}\wedge 0)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\leq\int(M_{n}-1\wedge 0)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\leq\int M_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=1,\forall n.

Due to the convergence of
(M_{n})_{n}
, it follows that
\lim\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=+\infty
. Note that

\displaystyle\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\sum_{A\in\xi^{n}}\mu(A)\log\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu_{L}(A)}

and therefore by the Gibbs property of
\mu_{L}
it holds

\displaystyle H_{\mu}(\xi^{n})=-\sum_{A\in\xi^{n}}\mu(A)\log\mu_{L}(A)-\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\leq E+n\Ptop(L)-\int L^{(n)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu-\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle\Rightarrow h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)=\inf_{n}\frac{H_{\mu}(\xi^{n})}{n}+\mu(L)\leq\frac{H_{\mu}(\xi^{n})}{n}+\frac{\int L^{(n)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu}{n}+\left(\mu(L)-\frac{\int L^{(n)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{E+\lVert\delta L\rVert-\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu}{n}+\Ptop(L).

For
n
large the quantity
\frac{E+\lVert\delta L\rVert-\int N_{n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu}{n}
is strictly negative, and therefore if
\mu
is singular with respect to
\mu_{L}
then
h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)<\Ptop(L)=h_{\mu_{L}}(\tau)+\mu_{L}(L)
.

Finally, one notes that if
\mu=\int\mu_{\alpha}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Psi(\alpha)
for some
\Psi\in{P}({P}_{\tau}(\Sigma))
, then

P_{\mu}(L)=\int P_{\mu_{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Psi(\alpha)
: this follows due to convexity of the metric entropy with respect to such decomposition, plus the fact that
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)\to\mu(L)
is convex and continuous (cf.
Proposition

5.7
). As a consequence, applying this to an ergodic decomposition of a given invariant measure, we get to conclude that
\sup_{\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)}\{h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)\}=\sup_{\mu\in{P}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{erg}}(\Sigma)}\{h_{\mu}(\tau)+\mu(L)\}
, where
{P}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{erg}}(\Sigma)\subset{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
denotes the set of ergodic invariant measures of
\tau
.

Recalling the basic fact that two ergodic invariant measures are either mutually singular or they coincide, using that
\mu_{L}
is ergodic and applying the previous reasoning we conclude that it is the unique equilibrium state.
∎

Remark 6.19
.

For the first part of the previous theorem one could use the work of Cao, Feng and Huang
[
56
]
, where they establish the variational principle for continuous sub-additive functions on compact metric spaces. The proof given above gives simultaneously the variational principle and the uniqueness of the equilibrium measure in our context.

We thus see that in fact, for
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
(equivalently, for
\phi\in\mathrm{Bow}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
or
\bm{B}\in\mathrm{QCB}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
) the corresponding measure
\mu_{L}
is canonically defined to its cohomology class. In fact, there is a one to one correspondence as we show below.

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
L_{1},L_{2}\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
be such that they have the same equilibrium state
\mu
, with
\mu(L_{1})=\mu(L_{2})
. Then, they are cohomologous.

Indeed, the hypothesis shows that the potentials associated to
L_{1},L_{2}
coincide, thus by
Corollary

6.17
we deduce that
(L^{(n)}_{1}-L^{(n)}_{2})_{n}
is uniformly bounded on a dense set of
\Sigma
. This is enough to show that they are uniformly bounded everywhere, and thus
L_{1}\sim L_{2}
.
∎

Remark 6.21
.

For Hölder functions
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
, the corresponding statement is due to Bowen (Proposition
4.5
in
[
14
]
). Bowen’s result includes the fact that that transfer function is Hölder. This can be deduced from the Corollary above, applying
[
49
]
to obtain a bounded continuous transfer function, and then using
[
17
]
to improve the regularity.

At this stage we have essentially proved the statements of
Theorem

5.2
. Indeed, defining
\psi_{L}=\varphi_{L}+\Ptop(L)
it follows by the previous inequality that
S_{n}\psi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
is uniformly close to
L(\widetilde{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}_{(n)})
. Moreover, given any other
\psi_{L}^{\prime}
with the weak Bowen property and satisfying the same, the quasicocycle
\bm{B}^{\psi_{L}^{\prime}}
is cohomologous to
\bm{B}^{L}
, and therefore it determines
L^{\prime}\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
cohomologous to
L
, which then has the same invariant measure
\mu_{L}
. Applying the previous Corollary we deduce that
\psi_{L}^{\prime}=\psi_{L}+u-u\circ\tau
for some
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)
, due to
Theorem

E
.

6.5.
The transfer operator associated to
L

Here we give another characterization of
\mu_{L}
. For this we introduce the transfer operator associated to
\varphi_{L}
, which will also be used later. Fix
p\in[1,+\infty]
and define
{L}_{\varphi_{L}}={L}_{L}:L^{p}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)\toit
by the formula

{L}_{L}\psi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\sum_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}e^{\varphi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}\psi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}).

The following is verified by direct computation.

Lemma 6.22

(Transference property)
.

Let
\psi\in L^{p}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)
and
\phi\in L^{q}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)
where
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
if
p<\infty
, and
q=\infty
is
p=\infty
. Then

(6.12)

\displaystyle{L}_{L}(\psi\cdot\phi\circ\tau)={L}_{L}\psi\cdot\phi.

We are interested in the action of the transpose of
{L}
when
p=\infty
. Consider
\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L})=\mathrm{Add}(\Sigma,{B}(\Sigma),\mu_{L})
the set of finitely additive measures on
(\Sigma,{B}(\Sigma))
that vanish on the null-sets of
\mu_{L}
. Due to the Yosida-Hewitt representation theorem we can identify
\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L})=L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)^{*}
; since
\mu_{L}(\Sigma)=1
(finite) it follows that
\mu_{L}\in\mathrm{Add}(\Sigma)
. We can then consider
{L}^{*}:\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L})\toit
the corresponding adjoint operator, with

\nu\in\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L}),\ \varphi\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)\Rightarrow{L}_{L}^{*}\nu(\varphi)=\nu({L}_{L}\varphi).

Lemma 6.23
.

It holds
{L}_{L}1=1
for
\mu_{L}\text{-a.e.}

Using that
{L}_{L}1=\sum_{a\in{A}}{L}_{L}1_{[a]}
and linearity we obtain

\displaystyle{L}_{L}1_{[a]}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle=R_{ax_{0}}e^{\varphi_{L}(ax_{0}x_{1}\cdots)}=\lim_{n}\frac{\mu_{L}([ax_{0}\cdots x_{n-1}])}{\mu_{L}([x_{0}\cdots x_{n-1}])}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{L}_{L}1(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\lim_{n}\sum_{a\in{A}}R_{ax_{0}}\frac{\mu_{L}([ax_{0}\cdots x_{n-1}])}{\mu_{L}([x_{0}\cdots x_{n-1}])}=1=1(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}),

for
\mu_{L}\text{-a.e.}
point.
∎

Corollary 6.24
.

It holds
{L}_{L}^{*}\mu_{L}=\mu_{L}
.

By the transference property it follows that for
\phi,\psi\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)

\displaystyle\int\phi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}=\int\phi\circ\tau\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}=\int\phi\circ\tau\cdot{L}_{L}1\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}=\int{L}_{L}\phi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{L}

and the claim follows.
∎

Remark 6.25
.

The same argument shows that if
\nu\in\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L})
is invariant under
{L}_{L}^{*}
, then it is
\tau
-invariant.

Corollary 6.26
.

If
\nu\in\mathrm{Add}(\mu_{L})
is
\sigma-
additive and
{L}_{L}^{*}
invariant, then
\nu=\mu_{L}
.

Previous remark and ergodicity of
\mu_{L}
.
∎

Now take
\psi\in\mathrm{Bow}^{\,}_{\mu_{L}}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma),\psi\sim\varphi_{L}
: by
Theorem

5.19
there exists
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)
so that
\varphi_{L}=\psi+u-u\circ T
. It follows that

\displaystyle 1={L}_{\varphi_{L}}(1)=\frac{{L}_{\psi}e^{u}}{e^{u}}

and
e^{u}
is an eigenvector of
{L}_{\psi}
corresponding to the eigenvalue
1
. Proposition
4.3
of
[
26
]
implies that in fact
e^{u}
is a simple eigenfunction of
{L}_{\psi}:L^{1}\!\left(\mu_{L}\right)\toit
.

6.6.
The potential for other reference measures

In order to address more precise statistical properties of a given quasimorphism (as the Central Limit Theorem), the construction of its associated potential has a major drawback, namely, it is defined only for its equilibrium measure
\mu_{L}
. In this part we remedy this: given some invariant measure
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
we construct an associated potential
\varphi_{L,\mu}\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu\right)

with the weak Bowen property, and so that
(S_{n}\varphi_{L,\mu})_{n\geq 1}
is cohomologous to
(L^{(n)})_{n\geq 1}
. Although this method also works for
\mu_{L}
, unlike the previous construction, the potentials obtained do not seem to be unique, thus preventing a unique characterization of
\varphi_{L}
.

Fix
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and consider the sequence of locally constant functions
\zeta_{n}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}L(x_{0}\ldots x_{k})-L(x_{1}\ldots x_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(L(x_{0}x_{1})-L(x_{1})+L(x_{0}x_{1}x_{2})-L(x_{1}x_{2})+\cdots L(x_{0}\ldots x_{n})-L(x_{1}\ldots x_{n})\right).

The following is clear.

Lemma 6.27
.

(\zeta_{n})_{n\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\Sigma)
is uniformly bounded:
\lVert\zeta_{n}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}\leq\lVert L\rVert
.

Let us now fix
T\in\mathbb{N}
and consider
n>T
.

Lemma 6.28
.

It holds

|S_{T}\zeta_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\cdots x_{T-1})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert(\frac{2T}{n}+1-\frac{T}{n})+\frac{T}{n}\lVert L|_{{A}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}.

Since
\zeta_{n}\circ\tau^{l}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}L(x_{l}\ldots x_{l+k})-L(x_{l+1}\ldots x_{l+k})
, one gets

\displaystyle S_{T}\zeta_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{T-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}L(x_{l}\ldots x_{l+k})-L(x_{l+1}\ldots x_{l+k})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}L(x_{0}\cdots x_{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}L(x_{k}\cdots x_{n+k})-\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}L(x_{k})-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}L(x_{T}\cdots x_{T+k})\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle|S_{T}\zeta_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\cdots x_{T-1})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{T}{n}\lVert L|_{{A}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}+\frac{1}{n}\left|\sum_{k=0}^{T-2}L(x_{0}\cdots x_{k})+L(x_{k+1}\cdots x_{n+k})-L(x_{0}\cdots x_{T-1})-L(x_{T}\cdots x_{T+n-2})\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\left|\sum_{k=T}^{n}L(x_{0}\cdots x_{k})-L(x_{0}\cdots x_{T-1})-L(x_{T}\cdots x_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert(\frac{2T}{n}+1-\frac{T}{n})+\frac{T}{n}\lVert L|_{{A}}\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle\!\ell^{\infty}}.

∎

To construct the potential we now argue as in Burkholder’s proof of Kingman subadditive ergodic theorem,
[
57
]
. The following is delicate result due to Komlós.

Theorem 6.29

(Komlos,
[
58
]
)
.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on a measurable space
(X,{B}(X))
and suppose that
(f_{n})_{n}\in L^{1}\!\left(\mu\right)
is uniformly bounded in norm. Then there exists
f\in L^{1}\!\left(\mu\right)
and a sub-sequence
(r(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
so that for
\mu\text{-a.e.}\ x\in X
it holds

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}f_{r(n)}(x)=f(x).

Proposition 6.30
.

For any
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
there exists
\varphi_{L,\mu}:D_{\mu}\to\mathbb{R}\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu\right)
so that

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in D_{\mu},n\in\mathbb{N}\Rightarrow|S_{n}\varphi_{L,\mu}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\ldots x_{n-1})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert.

In particular, if
\mu
has full support then
\varphi_{L,\mu}
has the weak Bowen property.

Applying Komlós’ theorem to
(\zeta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, let
\varphi_{L,\mu}:D_{\mu}\to\mathbb{R}
and
(r(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be as in its conclusion. Fixing
T\in\mathbb{N},\varepsilon>0
, for sufficiently large
n
one has that
\sup_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma}|S_{T}\zeta_{n}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\ldots x_{T-1})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert+\varepsilon
, therefore (since
\varphi_{L,\mu}
is limit of a convex combination of
(\zeta_{n})
),

\sup_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma}|S_{T}\varphi_{L,\mu}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\ldots x_{T-1})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert+\varepsilon.

This implies the first part, while the second is automatic.
∎

Remark 6.31
.

Since
(\zeta_{n})_{n}
are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu\right)
, we have

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}L(x_{0}\ldots x_{r(n)-1})-L(x_{1}\ldots x_{r(n)-1})=\varphi_{L,\mu}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

both
a.e.
and in
L^{p}\!\left(\mu\right)
for
1\leq p<\infty
.

Remark 6.32
.

The following generalization is useful to compare weak Bowen functions associated to different measures. Suppose that
(\mu_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
is given: then there exists a
\varphi:D\to\mathbb{R}
so that

(1)

\forall k,\mu_{k}(D)=1
.

(2)

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in D,n\in\mathbb{N}\Rightarrow|S_{n}\varphi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-L(x_{0}\ldots x_{n-1})|\leq\lVert\delta L\rVert.

To see this it suffices to apply the previous proposition to
\mu=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{k}}{2^{k}}
. It would be interesting to establish the existence of a cohomologous weak function defined for every invariant measure, but this remains as an open problem.

7.
Finer properties of the equilibrium measure associated to a quasimorphism

In this section we investigate the dynamical properties of the equilibrium state, and elucidate the structure of the set where the potential
\varphi_{L}
is well defined. We use this knowledge to deduce the Bernoulli property of the system
(\Sigma,\tau,\mu_{L})
.

This part is more dynamically flavored than others in the article, and contains results that are well known for the case of Hölder functions. For weak Bowen functions we need to give different proofs. These methods also reveal non-obvious structure of weak Bowen functions, and hence of quasimorphisms. It is also likely that the machinery that we present here will be applicable in other instances of thermodynamic formalism for non-continuous potentials, which is still a largely undeveloped area.

7.1.
The transpose quasimorphism and the natural extension

It will be important to work in the natural extension of the shift dynamical system, which can be canonically identified with the two-sided SFT
\widetilde{\Sigma}=\widetilde{\Sigma}(R)
. The canonical projection is
\pi:\widetilde{\Sigma}\to\Sigma,\pi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}
; as was explained in
Section

5
, it induces an isomorphism
{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})\ni\widetilde{\mu}\to\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
, preserving ergodicity.

Notation
.

For points
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
we use a dot to indicate the zero entry, i.e.

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}=(\cdots x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}\cdots x_{n}\cdots).

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma},k\leq 0\leq l
we write

[x_{k}x_{k+1}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}x_{1}\cdots x_{l}]=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}:y_{i}=x_{i},\ k\leq i\leq l\}.

Given
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
we denote
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}=(x_{n})_{n\geq 0},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}=(x_{n})_{n\leq 0}
, and write
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle
.

The space non-positive entries of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is also a one-sided shift space, which can be identified with the subshift of finite type
\Sigma(R^{\dagger})
corresponding to
R^{\dagger}
, the transpose of
R
. Indeed, we can write

\Sigma(R^{\dagger})=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}=(x_{n})_{n\leq 0}:R_{x_{n-1}x_{n}}=1,\forall n\geq 0\}
, and observe that in this notation the shift endomorphism is given by
(x_{n})_{n\geq 0}\mapsto(x_{n-1})_{n\geq 0}
: we denote this map by
\tau^{-1}
. The projection
\pi^{\dagger}:\widetilde{\Sigma}(R)\to\Sigma(R^{\dagger})
,

\pi^{\dagger}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=(x_{-n})_{n\geq 0}

semi-conjugates
\tau^{-1}:\widetilde{\Sigma}\toit
and
2
2

2

Here
\tau^{-1}:\widetilde{\Sigma}\toit
is the inverse of the shift map in
\widetilde{\Sigma}
.

\tau^{-1}:\Sigma(R^{\dagger})\toit
. It follows in particular that
{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})={P}_{\tau^{-1}}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
is in bijection with
{P}_{\tau^{-1}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})
, and therefore there exists a bijection
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)\to\mu_{\dagger}\in{P}_{\tau^{-1}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})
.

We now fix
L\in\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
and lift the measure
\mu_{L}
to obtain
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
. Let
\varphi_{L}:\Sigma_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
be the associated potential, where
\Sigma_{0}
is completely invariant and
\mu_{L}(\Sigma_{0})=1
. The lifted potential
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\varphi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+})
is defined in the full measure invariant set
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}=\pi^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})
. Observe that
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}
satisfies the weak Bowen property and only depends on the positive coordinates of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
is the (unique) equilibrium state for
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}
.

We use the following facts:

(1)

for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau}(\Sigma)
,
h_{\mu}(\tau)=h_{\widetilde{\mu}}(\tau)
;

(2)

for every
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
,

\widetilde{\mu}_{\bm{a}}(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}})=\mu_{\bm{a}}(\varphi_{L}).

This in turn implies, by density of measures supported on periodic orbits
[
59
]
and
Proposition

5.7
, that

(\forall\widetilde{\mu}\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})):\ \widetilde{\mu}(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}})=\mu(\varphi_{L})=\mu(L).

The result then follows from the variational principle (note that proof of
Theorem

6.18
applies without modifications to the system
\left(\widetilde{\Sigma},\tau,\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\right)
).
∎

Next we look at
\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
. For a word
\bm{a}=a_{1}\cdots a_{n}
write
\bm{a}^{\dagger}=a_{n}\cdots a_{1}
. Then
\dagger:{W}={W}(R)\to{W}^{\dagger}={W}(R^{\dagger})
induces a linear isomorphism
\dagger:\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})\to\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}^{\dagger})
(respectively,
\dagger:\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}))\to\mathrm{QM}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}^{\dagger}))
) by the formula

L^{\dagger}(\bm{a})=L(\bm{a}^{\dagger});

accordingly,
\lVert\delta L^{\dagger}\rVert=\lVert\delta L\rVert,\lVert L^{\dagger}\rVert=\lVert L\rVert
.

We define
\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}:\Sigma^{\dagger}_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
as before,

(7.1)

\displaystyle e^{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-})}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{L^{\dagger}}([x_{-n}\cdots\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}])}{\widetilde{\mu}_{L^{\dagger}}([x_{-n}\cdots\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{-1}])}

and consider its lift to
\widetilde{\Sigma}
,
\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-})
.

Lemma 7.2
.

\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
is the equilibrium state for
\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}
. Therefore
\pi^{\dagger}\widetilde{\mu}_{L}=\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
.

We argue as in the previous Lemma, using that

(1)

for every
\mu\in{P}_{\tau^{-1}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})
,
h_{\mu}(\tau^{-1})=h_{\widetilde{\mu}}(\tau^{-1})=h_{\widetilde{\mu}}(\tau)
.

(2)

For every
\bm{a}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W})
it holds that
\bm{a}^{\dagger}\in\mathrm{Fix}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}({W}^{\dagger})
and

\widetilde{\mu}_{\bm{a}^{\dagger}}(\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}})=\mu_{\bm{a}^{\dagger}}(\varphi_{L^{\dagger}})=\mu_{\bm{a}^{\dagger}}(L^{\dagger})=\mu_{\bm{a}}(L).

Hence,

\forall\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma}),\ \nu(\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}})=\nu(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}).

The result follows again by the variational principle and the uniqueness of the equilibrium measure for
\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}
.
∎

During the proof of
Lemmas

7.1
and
7.2
we established that
\forall\nu\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
it holds
\nu(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}})=\nu(\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}})
. For this reason:

Corollary 7.3
.

The functions
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}},\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}
are cohomologous in
L^{\infty}\!\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{L}\right)
: there exists
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{L}\right)
so that
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}=\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}+u-u\circ\tau
.

Convention
.

From now on
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}},\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}
are defined in the same invariant set of full
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
measure,
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}=\pi^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})\cap(\pi^{\dagger})^{-1}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}^{\dagger})
. The transfer function
u
is defined in the full
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
measure and invariant set
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
.

Remark 7.4
.

The previous Corollary evidences further the necessity of working with the coarser version if Livšic cohomology. Indeed, in
[
60
]
the authors give an example of a continuous Bowen potential
\varphi:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
for which the reverse measure
\mu_{\dagger}
is not associated to any continuous potential.

7.2.
The conditionals of
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}

Our next goal is to prove that
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
is equivalent to the product
\mu_{L}\times\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
, and give a dynamical interpretation of the transfer function
u
. First, we recall the basics of the theory of measurable partitions and disintegration of measures in the sense of Rohklin. Given a Borel probability space
(X,{B}(X),\mu)
and a family of partitions (by measurable sets)
(\mathrm{P}_{i})_{i\in I}
, its join is
\bigvee_{i\in I}\mathrm{P}_{i}=\sigma(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathrm{P}_{i})<{B}(X)
. A
\sigma
-algebra
\xi
is called measurable if there exists a countable family of finite partitions
(\mathrm{P}_{n})_{n}
so that
\xi=\bigvee_{n}\mathrm{P}_{n}
, while a partition
\mathrm{P}
is called measurable if the
\sigma
-algebra that it generates is measurable.

Theorem 7.5

(Rohklin)
.

If
\xi
is a measurable
\sigma-
algebra for
\mu
, there exists
X_{0}\subset X
of full measure, and a family
\{\mu_{x}^{\xi}\}_{x\in X}\subset{P}(X)
(called the conditionals of
\mu
with respect to
\xi
) so that for every
\psi\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu\right)
the map
x\in X_{0}\to\mu_{x}^{\xi}(\psi)
is a measurable version of
\mathbb{E}_{#1}[\psi|\xi]
. In particular,
\mu(\psi)=\int\left(\int\psi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu^{\xi}_{x}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(x)
.

Since
\xi
is measurable, for
\mu
-almost every
x\in X
the set
\xi(x)=\bigcap_{x\in A\in\xi}A
(called the atom of
\xi
containing
x
) is in
{B}(X)
, and
X_{0}
can be chosen so that
x\in X_{0}
implies

•

\mu_{x}^{\xi}(\xi(x))=1
,

•

y\in X_{0}\cap\xi(x)\Rightarrow\mu_{x}^{\xi}=\mu_{y}^{\xi}
.

Define
X_{\xi}
as the space of atoms of
\xi
and equip it with the final measure structure induced by the projection
p_{\xi}(x)=\xi(x)
. Due to the above considerations, the family of conditionals can also be thought of as a measurable map
(X/\xi,p_{\xi}\mu)\mapsto{P}(X)
sending
A=\xi(x)\to\mu^{\xi}_{A}=\mu^{\xi}_{x}
. The measure
\mu
is thus completely determined by
\left\{\mu^{\xi}_{A}\right\}_{A\in X/\xi}
and the quotient measure
\mu_{X/\xi}=p_{\xi}\mu
. We refer to
[
61
]
for further discussion.

Stable and unstable sets

For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
denote

(7.2)

\displaystyle W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\coloneqq\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\}

(7.3)

\displaystyle W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\coloneqq\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}\}

Definition 7.6
.

W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}),W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
are the local stable and unstable sets of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
, respectively.

These sets can be parametrized as follows. Fix
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
and let

\displaystyle p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\Sigma_{x_{0}}=\Sigma\cap[x_{0}]\to W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\rangle

\displaystyle p^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\Sigma^{\dagger}_{x_{0}}=\Sigma^{\dagger}\cap[x_{0}]\to W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle p^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle.

Both
p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}},p^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
are homeomorphisms.

Write
\xi^{u}=\{W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}
for the partition into local unstable sets: it holds that

•

\xi^{u}
is finer than
\tau\xi^{u}=\{\tau\left(\xi(\tau^{-1}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))\right):\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}
;

•

\bigvee_{n\leq 0}\tau^{n}\xi^{u}={B}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
;

•

\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\tau^{n}\xi^{u}=\{W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}=:{B}^{u}
, where
W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}:\lim_{n\to\infty}\distop(\widetilde{\Sigma},\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}){\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}=0\}
.

Similarly for the partition
\xi^{s}=\{W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}
, replacing
\tau
by its inverse. Next we compute the disintegration of
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
with respect to the partition
\xi^{u}
.

Notation
.

Denote
\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=(\widetilde{\mu}_{L})^{\xi^{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
, whenever is defined. For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma,\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
and
k\geq 0
we write

\displaystyle[y_{0}\cdots y_{k}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):z_{n}=y_{n},\forall n\leq k\}=p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}y_{1}\cdots y_{k}]).

Likewise if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
, we write

\displaystyle[y_{-k}\cdots y_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}):z_{n}=y_{n},\forall n\geq-k\}=p^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([y_{-k}\cdots y_{0}]).

If we now assume that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
it follows that

\displaystyle\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([y_{0}\cdots y_{k}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}y_{1}\cdots y_{k}])}{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}])}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}x_{0}y_{1}\cdots\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}y_{k}])}{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}])}

(7.4)

\displaystyle=\exp(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}))=\exp\left(S_{-k}\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\cdots x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}x_{0}y_{1}\cdots y_{k})\right)

with the convention that
S_{0}\varphi\equiv 0,S_{-k}\psi=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\psi\circ\tau^{-i}
. We remark that we have used the invariance of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
the guarantee the existence of the limit (cf.
Remark

6.15
): more generally, this permits to define
\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
whenever
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
belongs to the set
(\pi^{\dagger})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0}^{\dagger})
, which is saturated by the partition
\xi^{u}
. These unstable measures only depend on the past of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
, and we can safely write
\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}}
.

Corollary 7.7
.

The unstable disintegration can be defined on
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
.

Similarly for the stable disintegration we get,

\displaystyle\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([y_{-k}\cdots y_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([y_{-k}\cdots y_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}x_{1}\cdots x_{n}])}{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([x_{-n}\cdots x_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}])}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}y_{-k}\cdots y_{-1}x_{0}x_{1}\cdots x_{n}])}{\widetilde{\mu}_{L}([\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}x_{0}\cdots x_{n}])}

(7.5)

\displaystyle=\exp(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}))=\exp\left(S_{k}\varphi_{L}(y_{-k}\cdots y_{-1}x_{0}x_{1}\cdots x_{n}\cdots)\right).

It follows that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\mapsto\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is well defined for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
. Next we compute the behavior of these stable/unstable measures under iteration.

Corollary 7.8
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
it holds

(1)

if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\tau^{-1}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))
, then

\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau^{-1}\mu^{u}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})=\exp(-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})).

(2)

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\tau(W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\tau^{-1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))
, then

\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau\mu^{s}_{\tau^{-1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})=\exp(-\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})).

Compute

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}\mu^{u}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([y_{0}\cdots y_{k}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}})

\displaystyle=\mu^{u}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\left([y_{1}\cdots y_{k}]^{u}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\exp(S_{k-1}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau^{2}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}))=\exp(-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}))\cdot\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([y_{0}\cdots y_{k}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}})&x_{1}=y_{1}\\
0&x_{1}\neq y_{1}.\end{cases}

This shows that
\tau^{-1}\mu^{u}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\sim\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. By taking
k\mapsto\infty
and using the Radon-Nikodym theorem we obtain the claim in the first case. The second is analogous.
∎

It follows that we can write

\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\sum_{i\in{A}}R_{x_{0}i}1_{[x_{0}i]}e^{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}i)}\tau^{-1}\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}i}^{u}.

Holonomy.

Given
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
with
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
we define
\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}:W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\to W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})

\displaystyle\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle.

We call this map the
local unstable holonomy
between
W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
and
W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
. Note that

\forall\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}),\ \hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{s})=[z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}^{s}.

Now suppose that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
and let
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
. Then for every
k\geq 0
one has

\displaystyle\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\left([z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle))\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)-S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\right)\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\left([z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)

(7.6)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-i}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-i}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}\cdot\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\left([z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)

which if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}},\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
is equal to

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(u(\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}{\exp\left(u(\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}:\frac{\exp\left(u(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}{\exp\left(u(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}\cdot\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\left([z_{-k}\cdots z_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right).

Definition 7.9
.

The unstable Jacobian between
W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
and
W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
is the function

\mathrm{J}^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\prod_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}

for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
(whenever it exists).

By the computation above, and since
\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}
has the weak Bowen property, the measures
\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}},\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
are equivalent. The Radon-Nikodym theorem now implies that there exists a full
\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
measure set where the limit
\mathrm{J}^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})
exists.

Corollary 7.10
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
with
x_{0}=y_{0}
the measures
\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}},\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
are equivalent:
\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}=\mathrm{J}^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\cdot\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
, where
\mathrm{J}^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)
is bounded away from zero and infinity, with constants that do not depend on
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}
.

Similarly, the stable holonomy is the map
\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}:W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\to W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
defined by
\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle)=\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle
. As before, we compute for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

\displaystyle\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([x_{0}z_{1}\cdots z_{k}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})=\exp\left(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle)\right)

(7.8)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle)-S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle)\right)\cdot\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}z_{1}\cdots z_{k}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})

which if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}},\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
is equal to

(7.9)
