\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(u(\tau\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle))}{\exp(u(\tau\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle))}:\frac{\exp(u(\tau^{k+1}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle))}{\exp(u(\tau^{k+1}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{+}\rangle))}\cdot\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}z_{1}\cdots z_{k}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u}).

7.3.
The set of convergence of the unstable Jacobian

The previous
Corollary

7.10
guarantees that, if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
verify
x_{0}=y_{0}
, then for
\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
almost every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
the limit

\lim_{k\to\infty}S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}))-S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})

exists. For this, implicitly we have used that
\pi^{-1}(\Sigma_{0})
is saturated by the partition
\xi^{s}
. In principle we do not have means to guarantee that the same holds for the domain of the transfer function in order to deduce that
\lim_{k\to\infty}u(\tau^{-k}\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}))-u(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})
also exists. Nevertheless, by definition of disintegration we get the following:

Corollary 7.11
.

There exists a full measure invariant set
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
so that for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2},x_{0}=y_{0}
it holds

\exists\lim_{k\to\infty}u(\tau^{-k}\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}))-u(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\quad\mu^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\text{-a.e.}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})

In this part we analyze more carefully the previous convergence. For
\phi\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2})
let

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u}(\phi)=\int\mathbb{E}_{#1}[\phi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\cdot)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mu}_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}).

Then
\mathbb{P}^{u}
is a probability supported in the fiber bundle
\mathrm{E}=\left\{(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\right\}
. If
\mathrm{proj}^{(1)},\mathrm{proj}^{(2)}:\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}\to\widetilde{\Sigma}
denote the respective projections onto the first and second coordinates, we get in particular
\mathrm{proj}^{(1)}\mathbb{P}^{u}=\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
.

Define the probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{us}
on
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{3}
by the formula

\phi\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mu\mathopen{}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{3}),\ \mathbb{P}^{us}(\phi)=\int\mathbb{E}_{#1}[\phi(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\cdot)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\,\mathbb{P}^{u}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}).

Denote
\mathrm{proj}:\widetilde{\Sigma}^{3}\to\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}
the projection onto the first two coordinates. From
Corollary

7.11
we deduce:

Lemma 7.12
.

There exists a full
\mathbb{P}^{us}
measure set
\mathcal{D}^{3}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}^{3}
such that
\mathrm{proj}(\mathcal{D}^{3})=\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}^{2}
, and so for
(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\in\mathcal{D}^{3}
it holds

\exists\lim_{k\to\infty}u(\tau^{-k}\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}))-u(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}).

Consider the family of functions
v_{k}:\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}_{2}\to\mathbb{R}
,
v_{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})=u(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
.

Lemma 7.13
.

There exists a full
\mathbb{P}^{u}
measure set
\mathcal{D}^{2}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}
so that

(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\in\mathcal{D}^{2}\Rightarrow\exists v(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}):=\lim_{k\to\infty}v_{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})

Indeed,
\mathrm{proj}\mathbb{P}^{us}=\mathbb{P}^{u}
. The claim follows from this and the previous lemma.
∎

Our next goal is showing that
v
is the zero function.

Lemma 7.14
.

It holds that
v(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})=0
for
\mathbb{P}^{u}
-almost every pair
(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
.

Suppose not and denote
B=\{(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\in\mathcal{D}^{2}:|v(x,y)|>0\}
. Using Egoroff and Lusin’s theorems one deduces the existence of constants
\varepsilon,\eta>0
and
k_{0}
so that for
k\geq k_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}^{u}(\hat{B}_{k})\geq\eta
, where
\hat{B}_{k}=\{(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}):|v_{k}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})|>\varepsilon\}
. Let
\hat{C}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}^{2}
be a compact set so that
(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\mapsto u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})
is continuous and
\mathbb{P}^{u}(\hat{C})\geq 1-\eta
. Observe that by the definition of
\mathbb{P}^{u}
and the defining properties of disintegrations it follows that
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}(B)\geq\eta
, with
B=\mathrm{proj}^{(1)}\hat{B}
.

Take
\delta>0
for which
\distop_{C}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})<\delta\Rightarrow|u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})|<\varepsilon
, and let
k_{1}\geq k_{0}
so that

\sup\{\diam(\tau^{-k_{1}}W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})):\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}\}<\delta.

This implies in particular that
\hat{B}\cap\tau^{k_{1}}\times\tau^{k_{1}}(\hat{C})=\emptyset
, therefore
\mathbb{P}^{u}(\tau^{k_{1}}\times\tau^{k_{1}}(\hat{B}))<\eta
, which in turn implies

\widetilde{\mu}_{L}(\mathrm{proj}^{(1)}(\tau^{-k_{1}}\times\tau^{-k_{1}}(\hat{B}))=\widetilde{\mu}_{L}(\tau^{-k_{1}}B))<\eta,

which is a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 7.15
.

There exists
\mathcal{D}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}^{3}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}^{3}
of full
\mathbb{P}^{us}
measure so that
(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})\in\mathcal{D}
then

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}}{e^{u(\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)}}=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}{\exp\left(\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right)}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\exp\left(S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{+}\rangle)-S_{k}\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\tau^{-k}\langle\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{-},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{+}\rangle)\right).

Remark 7.16
.

The previous corollary reveals yet another some subtlety of functions with the (weak) Bowen property. For
\varphi
to have the weak Bowen property,
\sup_{n\geq 0}|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-\varphi(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})|<\infty
for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})
, it turns out that in fact
\exists\lim_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\varphi(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-\varphi(\tau^{-k}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})<\infty
for points
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}
chosen with respect to some fully supported measure in the same unstable sets.

7.4.
New conditionals and the transverse measure

We now compare the unstable disintegrations with
\mu_{L}
, and the the transverse measure with
\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
. The method is based on what is done in
[
62
]
for regular Hölder potentials (see also
[
63
]
), which has its origins in the work of Haydn
[
64
]
, Bowen-Ruelle
[
65
]
, and Ledrappier-Young
[
66
]
. Here, on the other hand, we are dealing with a more delicate situation, since we have to compare disintegrations corresponding to a priori different invariant measures, which typically implies that their domain of definition are mutually singular. Another difficulty comes from the fact that we want to construct the unstable disintegration everywhere by using a measure equivalent to
\mu_{L}
(which is defined on a different space). In the literature the identification between
\Sigma
and the local unstable manifold is sometimes not made explicit, which does not pose a serious problem since the Jacobian is defined everywhere.

For
i\in{A}
let
\mu_{L}^{i}=\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{[i]}\mu_{L}
. Recall the definition of the transfer operator given in
Section

6.5
.

Lemma 7.17
.

If
a\in{A}
,
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in\Sigma_{0}
it holds
{L}_{L}1_{[a]}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})=R_{ax_{0}}e^{\varphi_{L}(a\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}
.

Direct computation.
∎

From this we deduce:

Corollary 7.18
.

It holds
\tau\mu_{L}^{a}=\sum_{i}R_{ai}e^{\varphi_{L}(a\bullet)}\mu_{L}^{i}=\sum_{i}R_{ai}1_{[i]}e^{\varphi_{L}(a\bullet)}\mu_{L}
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
we define

(7.10)

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu_{L}^{x_{0}}.

This is a finite measure supported on
W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
, which moreover verifies:

Lemma 7.19
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0}
the measure
\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is equivalent to
\mu^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. Furthermore, the Jacobian is uniformly bounded from above and below by some constant independent of
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
.

This follows from (
7.4
) and the Gibbs property of
\mu_{L}
.
∎

Note that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}^{-}\Rightarrow\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}
. Given
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
for each symbol
i
with
R_{x_{0}i}=1
we choose
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}\in\tau(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))\cap[i]
.

Lemma 7.20
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
it holds
\tau\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\sum_{i}R_{x_{0}i}e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\circ\tau^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}=\sum_{i}R_{x_{0}i}e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\circ\tau^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{-}_{(i)}}
.

It is direct to check that
\tau\circ p^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\sum_{i}1_{[x_{0}i]}\cdot p_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}^{u}\circ\tau
, while

p_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}^{u}\left(\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{[i]}e^{\varphi_{L}(x_{0}\bullet)}\mu_{L}\right)=e^{\varphi_{L}(x_{0}\bullet)\circ(p_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}^{u})^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\circ\tau^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}.

Hence, by
Corollary

7.18
,

\displaystyle\tau\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=(\sum_{i}\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{[x_{0}i]}\cdot p_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}^{u}\circ\tau)\mu_{L}^{x_{0}}=\sum_{i}R_{x_{0}i}e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\circ\tau^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}.

∎

For
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
the function
W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\cap\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\ni\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\to e^{-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}
is in
L^{\infty}\!\left(\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)
, therefore

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:=e^{-u}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}

(7.12)

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}

are well defined finite measures on
W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
. Our next goal is proving that
\{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\}
verifies

\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u}=\frac{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))}=\frac{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))}.

We start analyzing the behavior of
\{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\},\{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\}
under iteration.

Lemma 7.21
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
it holds

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}\nu_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}\exp(-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\tau^{-1}\left(\sum_{i}R_{x_{0}i}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}\right)

\displaystyle\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\tau^{-1}\left(\sum_{i}R_{x_{0}i}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}\right).

Write
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}=\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
. By
Lemma

7.20
we have
\tau\left(1_{[x_{0}i]}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right)=e^{-\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}\circ\tau^{-1}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}_{(i)}}
, hence in particular
\tau^{-1}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}=1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}e^{-\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{-1}\left(e^{-u}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}\right)=1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}e^{-u\circ\tau-\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}e^{-u-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}e^{-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}.

∎

The above lemma allows us to extend each
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
(for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
) to a Radon measure on
W^{\mathrm{u}}(x)
with some quasi-invariance property.

Lemma 7.22
.

There exists a family of measures
\left\{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right\}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}}
, where
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is a Radon measure on
W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
satisfying:

(1)

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\tau^{-n}\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{-S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
.

(2)

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
implies
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\lim_{n}e^{S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}
.

Fix
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
, and for
n\geq 0
write
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(n)}=\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
and
\nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\coloneqq e^{-S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(n)})}\tau^{n}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(n)}}
. It follows from the previous Lemma that this family of measures satisfies
\nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\nu^{(n-1)}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
on
\tau^{n-1}W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(n-1)})
, for every
n\geq 1
, and hence
\lim_{n}\ \nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is a well defined Radon measure on
\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\tau^{n}W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(n)})=W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
, which coincides with
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
on
W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
. From its definition, it is clear that
\nu_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\tau\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. The last part follows by noticing that if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})
, there exists some
k\geq 0
so that
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(k)}}=\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}^{(k)}}
, and then using the quasi-invariance.
∎

We obtain a similar statement for the measures
\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. We remark the dependence on the base point of these measures, even for points in the same local unstable manifold.

Lemma 7.23
.

There exists a family of measures
\left\{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\right\}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}}
, where
\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is a Radon measure on
W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
satisfying:

(1)

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\tau^{-n}\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{-S_{n}\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
.

(2)

\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in W^{\mathrm{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
implies
\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}\lim_{n}e^{S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\tau^{-n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}
.

Use the previous Lemma and define
\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
globally as
e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. Since
\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\widetilde{\varphi_{L}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})+u(\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
, the first equality follows. The others are direct from this.
∎

Now we understand the quasi-invariance properties of these measures, and we can compute how they transform under holonomy.

Corollary 7.24
.

Suppose that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
. Then for every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\cap W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\cap\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\left(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\cap W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})\right)
it holds

\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})-u(\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}.

It is no loss of generality to suppose
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}=\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}
. Observe that

\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})=\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}^{u})=e^{S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([x_{n}]^{u}_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}),

and likewise
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})=e^{S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{n}]_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})
. Since

S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-S_{n}\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-(u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})),

we get

\frac{\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})}{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})}=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}\left(e^{u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\frac{\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{n}]_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}^{u})}{\nu_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{n}]_{\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})}\right).

The term in parentheses can be seen to converge to one: indeed

•

\lim_{n\to\infty}u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-u(\tau^{n}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=0
due to
Lemma

7.14
, as we are assuming that
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}
, and

•

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u})}{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([x_{0}\ldots x_{n}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}^{u})}=1
, again by the same Lemma and arguing as in its proof (use Egoroff’s theorem with respect to the reference measure
\mu_{L}
).

By to the Lebesgue-Radon-Nikodym theorem we conclude the claim.
∎

The transverse measure

It is time to deal with the transverse measure. For
a\in{A}
let
\mu_{L^{\dagger}}^{a}=\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{[a]}\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
, and if
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
consider the measures

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=p^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu_{L^{\dagger}}^{x_{0}}

\displaystyle\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:=\sum_{j}R_{jx_{0}}\tau\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{j}}

where
\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{(j)}\in[jx_{0}]_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{s}
. It is easy to see that
\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
(which is completely analogous to
\eta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
) gives the transverse measure of the unstable disintegration on the rectangle that contains
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
. Correspondingly, the definition of
\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
is made in order to compatibilize the dynamics of
\tau
and
\tau^{-1}
, in particular, to guarantee the following lemma.

Lemma 7.25
.

For every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}
it holds
1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}\tau^{-1}\theta^{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
.

By definition,
1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}\tau^{-1}\theta_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. Arguing analogously as in
Corollary

7.18
one verifies that
\tau\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{j}}=1_{[jx_{0}]}e^{-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
, which leads us to

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\sum_{jx_{0}}e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\tau\zeta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{j}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\Rightarrow 1_{[x_{0}x_{1}]}\tau^{-1}\theta_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}}\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}.

∎

Remark 7.26
.

When defining the product structure
\Sigma^{\dagger}_{x_{0}}\times\Sigma_{x_{0}}\xrightarrow{\langle\bullet,\bullet\rangle}[x_{0}]\cap\widetilde{\Sigma}
, one could instead consider a different structure preserving map, changing the first factor for
\bigcup_{j/R_{jx_{0}}=1}[j]
, which also parametrizes
W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
. The measure
\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
corresponds to
\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
in these coordinates.

We keep proceeding in like manner as before: for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}
let

(7.13)

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{u\circ\tau}\theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}.

It follows that
\{\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\}
can be extended to a family of Radon measures on stable sets, verifying the quasi-invariance condition

(7.14)

\tau^{-1}\hat{\theta}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{\varphi_{L}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\hat{\theta}.

Proposition 7.27
.

Let
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}
with
x_{0}=y_{0}
. Then
\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=e^{u-u\circ\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}\hol^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}
.

Completely analogous to
Corollary

7.24
, using the previous quasi-invariance property of the family.
∎

The product structure of
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}

We now use the families
\{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\},\{\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}:\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\}
to construct a measure on the full two-sided shift. For each
a\in{A}
choose
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}=\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}^{a}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\cap[a]
and define the measure

\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}=\int\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\ \theta_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})

This is a well defined measure on
[a]
of full support, therefore
\mathrm{m}=\sum_{a\in{A}}\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}^{a}}
has full support in
\widetilde{\Sigma}
.

Lemma 7.28
.

If
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\cap[a]
then
\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}=\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}
.

Indeed, by
2)
of
Lemma

7.23
and
Proposition

7.27
we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}=\int_{W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}})}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\int_{W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{\prime})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{\prime})=\int_{W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}})}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}^{\prime}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}=\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle z}}{\underline{\textstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptstyle z}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle z}}}.

∎

We write
\mathrm{m}^{a}=\mathrm{m}^{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}
, where
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2}\cap[a]
.

Corollary 7.29
.

The measure
\mathrm{m}
is
\tau
invariant.

Consider
U=[w_{-k}\cdots w_{-1}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}w_{0}w_{1}\cdots w_{l}]
:
\tau(U)=[w_{-k}\cdots w_{-1}w_{0}\mathord{\bm{\ldotp}}w_{1}\cdots w_{l}]
. By the previous corollary we can write

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}^{w_{1}}(\tau U)

\displaystyle=\int_{[w_{-k}\cdots w_{0}w_{1}]^{s}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}}\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\tau[w_{0}w_{1}\cdots w_{l}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\ \hat{\theta}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})

which together with
1)
of
Lemma

7.23
gives

\displaystyle=\int_{[w_{-k}\cdots w_{0}w_{1}]^{s}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}}e^{-\varphi_{L}(\tau^{-1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}\hat{\nu}_{\tau^{-1}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\mathchoice{\text{1}}{\text{1}}{\scalebox{0.7}{\text{1}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{1}}}_{[w_{0}\cdots w_{l}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}}\circ\tau^{-1}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\theta}_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}).

Using
Equation

7.14
we finally get

\displaystyle=\int_{[w_{-k}\cdots w_{0}]^{s}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}([w_{0}\cdots w_{l}]^{u}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle w}}{\underline{\textstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptstyle w}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle w}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\mathrm{m}^{w_{0}}(U).

Sets
U
of the previous type form a basis for the topology of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
, hence
\tau^{-1}\mathrm{m}=\mathrm{m}
.
∎

Let
\hat{\mathrm{m}}=\frac{\mathrm{m}}{\mathrm{m}(\widetilde{\Sigma})}\in{P}_{\tau}(\widetilde{\Sigma})
. By definition, its unstable disintegration is given by normalizing the measures
\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
(equivalently, normalizing the measures
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
). Note that
\hat{}\mathrm{m}\ll\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
, and since the later is ergodic we conclude:

Corollary 7.30
.

\widetilde{\mu}_{L}=\hat{\mathrm{m}}
. In particular, for
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
almost every
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
it holds
\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u}=\frac{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))}=\frac{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}{\hat{\nu}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))}
.

The fact that
u\in L^{\infty}\!\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{L}\right)
implies:

Corollary 7.31
.

The measure
\widetilde{\mu}_{L}
is equivalent to
\mu_{L}\times\mu_{L^{\dagger}}
, with uniformly bounded Radon-Nikodym derivative.

The set of definition of the potential

Denote

c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}=\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})).

This defines a measurable function on the
u
-saturated set
(\pi^{\dagger})^{-1}(\Sigma_{0}^{\dagger})
.

By
Corollary

7.24
and (
7.8
) we get that, for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{2},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}\in W^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
implies

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}}{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}^{u}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}^{u}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\hol^{\mathrm{s}}_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}},\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}}{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}e^{u\circ\tau(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u\circ\tau(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}=e^{u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})-u(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})}

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}}{c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}}=e^{\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})-\widetilde{\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})}

which together with the quasi-invariance property of
\nu_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
yield

\displaystyle\nu_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}(\tau W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}))=\nu_{\tau\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}}}(\tau W^{\mathrm{u}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle y}}{\underline{\textstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptstyle y}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle y}})).

It follows that for every
a\in{A}
there exists some constant
E_{a}\in\mathbb{R}
so that for
\mu_{L^{\dagger}}\text{-a.e.}\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}
,

\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})=\log c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}+E_{a}

One sees that defining the value of
\varphi_{L^{\dagger}}(\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}})
is equivalent to define the normalizing constant
c_{\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}}
. for
\mathchoice{\underline{\displaystyle x}}{\underline{\textstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptstyle x}}{\underline{\scriptscriptstyle x}}\in\Sigma^{\dagger}
. Similar considerations apply to the function
\varphi_{L}
.

7.5.
Bernoulli property

We now reap the consequences of our work. For that, we need some definitions from abstract Ergodic Theory. Let
(X,{B}(X),\mu)
be a Borel probability space and
T:X\toit
a (measure preserving) automorphism. If
{F}\subset{B}(X)
is a
\sigma-
algebra or a partition by measurable sets, we write

-\infty\leq k\leq l\leq\infty\Rightarrow{F}_{k}^{l}=\bigvee_{i=k}^{l}T^{-i}{F}.

When
{F}
is a finite partition generating
{B}(X)
, one considers the measure
\nu
obtained extending

\nu(A\cap B):=\mu(A)\mu(B)\quad A\in{F}_{-\infty}^{0},B\in{F}_{0}^{\infty}.

Recall that
{F}
is a generator for
T
if
{F}_{-\infty}^{\infty}={B}(X)
.

Definition 7.32

(Ledrappier)
.

The process
(X,T)
is quasi-Bernoulli if
\nu
is equivalent to
\mu
.

A counterpart is given by the notion of Weak Bernoulli process of Ornstein and Friedmann.

Definition 7.33
.

The partition
{F}
is said to be Weak Bernoulli if for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
so that for every
n\geq 0

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in{F}^{n}_{0}\\
B\in{F}_{-N-n}^{-N}\end{subarray}}|\mu(A\cap B)-\mu(A)\mu(B)|<\varepsilon.

In this case
(T,{B}(X))
is said to be a Weak Bernoulli process.

Theorem 7.34

(Ledrappier,
[
67
]
)
.

For a generator
{F}
,
(X,T)
is quasi-Bernoulli if and only if
{F}
is a Weak Bernoulli partition.

Theorem 7.35

(Ornstein and Friedmann,
[
68
]
)
.

If
(X,T)
